# THE FP COHERENCE FOLD — one read of the whole corpus, the laws it was missing, the conflicts between its volumes ruled, and every volume amended in place

## Compiled on the owner's word of 2026-10-16 ("read them comprehensively, refine them, and then can you refine them as a whole to make them completely cohesive and coherent?" + "complete with adversarial review") from thirteen whole reads of the FP corpus against the code of record `origin/master` @ `00b648c9d`, and carried through the FP final architecture program (kit `settlement-engine-kits/fp-final-a73343ed-2026-10-17/`); it mints no flag of its own; nothing scheduled.
## CONSOLIDATED 2026-10-23 from the bracketed text of record at `1beabdc85` — the review record (the old header's history, §8 WHAT WAS AMENDED WHERE and §9 THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW) is in git and in `settlement-engine-kits/fp-final-a73343ed-2026-10-17/history/FOLD-review-record.md`; the corpus read's receipts are the kit `settlement-engine-kits/fp-coherence-2fc13d59-2026-10-16/findings/`.
## THE CONSTITUTION'S COMPANION: requirements 1–14 are `DESIGN_FP_SPINE.md` §1, and 15–24's text of record is §1 here. The OWNER's rows are marked THE OWNER'S / AN OWNER ROW (§7); ⭐ marks an owner-signed shape or a volume's owner row; every ruling is vetoable by one owner clause (§7).

---

## THE READER'S GUIDE

1. WHAT THIS IS. The companion of the FP constitution: one fold of the whole FP corpus. It states the requirements the spine was missing (15–24, §1), the status of record of every volume (§2), the conflicts between volumes and their rulings (§3), the one clock law (§4), the vocabulary register (§5), the felt chains and their order (§6) and the rows ruled for the owner (§7).
2. WHAT IT OWNS, AND WHOSE IT IS. The text of record of requirements 15–24; the status of record for every volume (§2), which replaces each landed volume's own header — the program's volumes are read from their own texts; the CLOSURE of the engagement registry — which kinds exist, who owns each, and that an unregistered kind cannot resolve (§3.4); the vocabulary register (§5); the chain inventory C0, C0.5, C1..C22 and THE ORDER NOTE (§6); the OD-FC rows (§7). Its rulings are taken by delegation under the owner's words of 2026-10-16 ("i want you to rule on the decisions. i give my permission now and for whatever is emergent") and 2026-10-17 ("I defer all judgement and decisions to you"), each vetoable by one owner clause; the rows the owner keeps are marked THE OWNER'S / AN OWNER ROW (§7) and are the owner's; ⭐ marks an owner-signed shape or a volume's owner row.
3. WHAT IT RULES. Ownership gaps between programs, collisions with a landed law or a later owner word, and registry, spelling and address conflicts (§3.1–§3.3; each row gives the conflict with its receipts, the ruling, and what is amended where); the clock's cure path and its walker (§4); the default order in which the chains are built (each chain's own read may overturn it; §6); the owner rows (§7).
4. WHAT IT REGISTERS. Every engagement kind that requirement 24 makes a hearing (§3.4) and every closed word of the corpus (§5). The fold holds the closed lists and the one-meaning rule; the OWNING volume's row is of record for a kind's verdict words, default, tilt and WHERE, and for a ladder's words.
5. HOW TO USE §3.4. Before a volume mints, answers or reads an engagement, find its kind there — its owner, CLIMAX or STANDING, its verdict words, its WHERE. A kind that is not on the list may not resolve; a new kind enters by a row added in the wave that builds it.
6. HOW TO USE §5. Before naming anything, look the word up there: one word, one meaning per surface; a LANDED token never renames; an unlanded name that collides renames unless three or more volumes key on it; prose disambiguates always; a new closed ladder is minted into §5 with a homonym check (requirement 21 (d)).
7. WHAT IT READS. Every FP volume as it stands — the program's fourteen consolidated volumes (THE REALM'S PAST born consolidated), the landed volumes with their dated brackets, the spine, CN and the owner's briefs — the map module's host documents, and the code of record by symbol: the corpus read at `00b648c9d`, the program's measurements at master `81f1cd963`. Volumes cite it by number ("fold §3.3.18", "fold §5", "OD-FC-46"), and every number here is stable.
8. THE WORDS NOT TO CONFUSE. A HEARING (requirement 24: an engagement held on the docket for the DM's week) is not the landed majors pause, which is the DM's own switch; the DM's forced outcome on a hearing is a VERDICT, never a "decree" (the landed Decree Registry's word); a bare "chain n" is FP-40's (§5); every other chain is named with its qualifier — this fold's coherence chains of §6 as C0..C22 ("chain C17"), CN's machinery census rows as "CN's census row C17", and the vassal chain and any cause chain by those names.

**Status: THE FOLD IS LAW WHERE IT RULES AND A ROUTING WHERE IT ROUTES.** Its rulings are vetoable by one owner clause; its owner rows (§7) decide nothing on silence. Nothing here schedules a build: the owner's standing order (UI + prose release first; "everything else after"; the EC dispatch gate "pushed and deployed") binds.

**Evidence class.** Every substrate statement is a READ at the code of record it names — `00b648c9d` for the corpus read, master `81f1cd963` for the program's measurements — CONFIRMED in the estate's sense; a statement marked EXECUTED was run, not only read, at the code of record it names. Where a reader reasoned rather than read, the read's file says PLAUSIBLE and this fold carries the word.

## §0 READING ORDER AND THE SHAPE OF WHAT WAS FOUND

Read: this volume §1 (the constitutional amendment) → §2 (the status truth
table — the state of record for every volume; it replaces each landed
volume's header, and the program's volumes are read from their own texts) → §3 (the
cross-program conflicts, ruled) → §4 (the clock) → §5 (the vocabulary
register) → §6 (the felt core) → §7 (the owner rows). Then the spine, then any
family. §8 (what was amended where) and §9 (the adversarial review) point at
the review record.

What the corpus read found at `00b648c9d`:

**The corpus is three things, not one.** A LIT CORE (pact formation and its
compliance, the faith unseating ladder, believed scarcity and conditions, the
seat's errand spine built dark, the cascade braid built dark, the causal
headline built dark); a BUILT-BUT-DARK MIDDLE (information and espionage almost
entire, TR-1..3, the commons voice, habit's substrate, WY-1); and an UNBUILT
RIM (populations entire, interior entire, wayfare beyond its first wave, faith
beyond the unseating, TR-4..8, war circulation, the epoch, EC). No volume's
header tells the truth about which third it is in; fourteen say "nothing
scheduled, every wave dark" and two of those families light in the default
preset.

**The worst defects found are mechanisms whose felt sentence cannot fire**, not
stale line numbers: the lure four families count on cannot be commissioned
(no caller for `lureCommission`); faith pacts cannot cross in any shipped
preset (`believedDevotionEnabled` is lit nowhere); the migration menu is
empty in every shipped world (nothing writes the route network), and lighting
routes beside demographics would cut every settlement's food imports to a
quarter with no sentence naming why; `mutual_defense` is wired to nothing;
the two-sided negotiation the spine calls settled law was never built (the
responder values alone); GRAMMAR lit without its own done-gate ever started;
HB's flagship, the owner's own example sentence, is not expressible at the
war chooser; the W-OPS design governs espionage from outside every volume.

**Three structural problems sit above every family and are this fold's real
job:** the clock (about a dozen modules read the one interval table, about
sixty constants in forty-five modules are hand-keyed, two wrong by their own
comment, and the walker the constitution promised was never built); the
ownership gaps between programs (the trade executors claimed by two programs
and built by neither, a commons grievance composite with five programs adding
terms and no owner of its budget, a coupling registry that minted pair ids the
design never had); and the constitution's own silences (no law ties a
mechanism's clock to how far a DM advances, none requires a wave to declare
what world it needs, none requires a constant to come from evidence, none
requires a reported erratum to be applied).

## §1 THE CONSTITUTIONAL AMENDMENT — requirements 15 through 24 (ruled under the owner's full delegation; each vetoable by one owner clause; the one the owner must set is marked)

The twelve requirements plus 13 and 14 stand verbatim in `DESIGN_FP_SPINE.md` §1.
Requirements 15–24 below are the constitution's text of record; the spine's §1
carries them after requirement 14. They are not new philosophy; each names a
rule the corpus already needed and systematically failed (the corpus read of
the spine, its §12, confirmed by every family read).

**REQUIREMENT 12 AND THE PUSH BUDGET** (§7 OD-FC-52; by delegation, vetoable). Requirement 12 (THE PACING GOVERNOR IS THE EDITOR), whose text of record is the spine's §1, carries THE PUSH BUDGET, written at the spine's requirement 12: one causal headline (SP-6) and a bounded handful of beats PUSHED per advance, everything else the chronicle; effects folded under causes (CW-1); beats ranked by focus; the docket surface a count and the top three by stakes; an envelope in every chain's experience read (DRAFT ≤ 8 lines per week-advance, ≤ 20 for an age receipt, UNMEASURED).

**THE OWNER'S DOCTRINES OF 2026-10-18** bind every requirement below and every volume. Their text of record is the spine's requirements 1 and 4 (both requirements are the spine's) and the four §7 rows OD-FC-67..70: (1) THE WORLD HOSTS THE CAMPAIGN, NEVER COMPETES WITH IT — "and remember, this does not compete with the campaign, it just provides a living world to host a campaign and story in it." (01:31), beside the owner's 2026-10-17 word "Think of this tool as the DMs tool not the party … We only supply setting and consequence"; (2) PURPOSE ONLY BY THE DM, MENACES NEVER EMERGENT — "well they have no purpose or destiny unless directed by the user, but if left alone, then absolutely correct. by the way, menaces are DM authored only, never organically emergent." (01:33); (3) MACRO OVER MICRO — "We're keeping a lot of things at the macro level, not the micro in accordance with what matters which is what happens for the dungeon master and the table." (06:32); (4) NO ENGINE MORTALITY CLOCK for named persons — "in fantasy settings, there are longed lived or immortal species and characters. this allows the DM to decide" (05:57); (5) MEDIEVAL FOR MILLENNIA — "We're keeping this strictly to the medieval and in the fantasy setting these last for millennia's on end." (06:32). None of the five changes a ruling below; each is a test every ruling must already pass.

**THE ORDINARY LIFE IS THE DM'S READING OF THE DOSSIER** — the owner's DOCTRINE (2026-10-20 12:50 CDT), verbatim: "ordinary people's lives are teh view of the DM and is the main point of the settlemetn dossier at large. We are not going to be able to give full depth to thousands to hundreds of thousands of NPCs per settlement, we only focus on named NPCs as tey impact the settlement. even military units nad bandats and beasts affect the states of the settlement which is then interpreted by the DM into how they want ot portray the ordinary day to day life." Beside MACRO OVER MICRO and THE WORLD HOSTS THE CAMPAIGN (above): the engine deepens NAMED NPCs as they impact the settlement and moves the SETTLEMENT'S STATES; the unnamed thousands are the DM's portrayal, read from the dossier; military units, bodies and beasts matter through the states they move. CONSEQUENCES (ruled by delegation, vetoable; §7 OD-FC-86): (a) every FP mechanism's felt output terminates in a SETTLEMENT STATE (a ledger the dossier renders) or a NAMED NPC's DEED — the Evidence Law's "what effect" is a state the dossier can show; (b) THE WIRING CENSUS (§7 OD-FC-79) counts a mechanism WIRED only when its reader chain ends in such a state or deed — a chain that ends in machinery no desk reads is a DEAD END; (c) a finding that asks for depth on unnamed people (ordinary lives, crowds as persons, a mortality clock for the unnamed) is CLOSED by doctrine, never a gap; (d) the dossier program (the owner's UI + prose release) is the ordinary life's one surface — the FP volumes supply its states and its named deeds, never its prose. The program's later volumes stand on it: THE FISCAL SEAT moves settlement states (the hand on the mint, the commons' rung — `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_FS.md` §9), THE OPPORTUNITY mints named deeds (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_OP.md` §18), THE MOVEMENT GRAMMAR and THE WAY OF WAR move only the states their contacts and the landed choosers already move (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_MG.md` §18, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_WS.md` §16); POP's commons stay "nobody" (FS §10). Mirrored into `DESIGN_FP_SPINE.md` §1 at requirement 1.

15. **THE BINDING-SCOPE LAW.** Every FP volume, present and future — the
    thirteen the compiled fold names, the later programs (EC, MF, LG, PG, CN,
    W-SEAT, W-OPS, W-COIN, W-FAITH where they touch an FP seam) and their
    successors — is bound by requirements 1–24. A volume that declares itself
    outside says so in its header with the reason. (At the corpus read the spine bound "the
    six program volumes and the coupling volume" by name, and nine later
    volumes cited it zero times.)

16. **THE FIRST-VISIBLE-LATENCY LAW** (THE OWNER'S TO SET — ruled by delegation in §7 OD-FC-1: a product rule about what a DM must see is the owner's).
    Every mechanism declares, beside its clock, its FIRST-VISIBLE
    LATENCY in DM advances — the DM's advance unit is week / month / season /
    year — and a one-month advance must surface at least the mechanism's
    fast-layer sign (a band word, a beat, a dossier line); the slow verdict is
    named separately. A mechanism whose first-visible latency is a year or
    more is built only behind one that is felt in a month, or behind the epoch
    program. A wave whose FIRST-VISIBLE line fails its experience read does not
    LIGHT: its lighting unit is refused until the sign fires. (The four experience reads of 10-02 found the lit layers "nearly
    silent at the table"; the family reads found most felt drama at the
    seasonal or yearly clock.)

    **WHAT IS SHOWN — the owner's three words of 2026-10-17, verbatim.** (1) "Remember that we are building something that is mostly going to be behind the scenes in regard to the simulator. The DM wants emergent experience that only when scrutinized upon shows coherence. Not the other way around we’re not trying to show the full machinery." — so every 'first-visible sign' and 'felt sentence' is an OUTCOME in the world's voice (what happened to whom), never the machinery (bands, weights, odds, holds, mechanisms); the WHY is available on SCRUTINY (the Why panel, the causal walk, receipts) when the DM asks, never pushed onto the surface. Three layers: SURFACE (emergent outcomes) · SCRUTINY (coherence on demand) · MACHINERY (never shown). (2) "Caravan should be shown on the map and condition words yes. But as toggles on the Caravan piece. What I’m trying to say is is that things that can build immersion should be shown. Things that are just very complex should not be unless viewed upon intentionally." — the test is IMMERSION versus COMPLEXITY: what builds immersion IS shown, behind toggles (caravans on the map as a layer; condition words on the caravan's own piece; menaces, borders and the like); what is merely complex is shown ONLY when the DM looks on purpose (the drill-down). The machinery is not forbidden from view; it is never pushed. (3) "Well, the point is to produce outcomes at the DM can’t predict (that’s the point for the whole simulator, emulating dwarf fortress), but still remains completely legible and coherence when scrutinized" — UNPREDICTABILITY IS THE GOAL, never a defect: an outcome the DM could not have foreseen is the product working (the Dwarf Fortress standard); the test is the other half, that every outcome is COMPLETELY legible and coherent when scrutinized (every link named, every change carrying its reason, no runaway that reads as a bug). Reviews never cite surprise as a risk; they cite illegibility, incoherence and runaways. The owner, the same minute, verbatim: "That’s also why there’s undo button and that the dungeon master still has control over everything" — surprise is SAFE because the DM can undo any advance, edit any state (requirement 14) and decide any hearing (requirement 24); the engine is never made tamer to protect the DM's story.

    **THE SLOW SYSTEMS' SIGNS** (§7 OD-FC-55; by delegation, vetoable). This requirement binds every SLOW mechanism without exception — the week the crown's hold moves, a line says so (PL-2); the season a rite spreads, the dossier says so (PL-11's planting line, §4); the slow verdict names its planting. The fast actors are the couplings that bring slow systems to the table (a kingdom-wide ban met at the party's gate, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_CH.md` §11; a saint walking in, CH-3) — the reason chains C13 THE PARTY and C12 THE CHAMPIONS sit early in §6's order (THE ORDER NOTE). The age receipt (chain C8; OD-FC-51) LEADS with the slow systems: the crown, the faiths, the economy's drift.

17. **THE TIER DECLARATION LAW.** Every wave carries a TIER line: aspatial-safe
    / spatial canon / belief-recording infoMode / host flags it needs, and its
    DEGRADED ARM in the lesser tier — exactly as requirement 13 forces an
    Alignment line. The paid-tier sense is declared where it governs the
    felt sentence: the SIMULATOR is premium (the owner, 10-16); the DEMO CAP
    (anonymous = town, free = village + town) is the owner's "completely
    temporary" pre-launch cap with its launch switch beside U16 (FP-77), never a
    design tier. (Belief, and therefore all of INFO/ES/TR-3/HB-6..8 and
    EC-16/18, runs only under spatial canon with a non-omniscient infoMode;
    faith runs only on deity-bearing premium worlds; no volume says so at the
    top.)

    **CANONIZED ≠ PREMIUM** (by delegation, vetoable). The WORLD CANON is geography, a world fact, not the simulator. The map module's H22 (the kit's `refs/HOST-REQUIREMENTS-2026-10-18.md`, the owner's attachment of 2026-10-18: "Every new campaign on a map-module world, whatever its tier, adopts the canon as it is created"; the map program has since moved that canon to the campaign's first advance, THE GRACE RULE, OD-FC-136) is therefore COMPATIBLE with this requirement: the premium sense governs the SIMULATOR's lanes (the hearing week, and every canon-gated lane where the simulator runs), never whether a world has its geography. A TIER line's "spatial canon" names the world's geography tier, never an entitlement; on a map-module world the canon is adopted at the head of the campaign's FIRST ADVANCE over the seats as they then stand (THE GRACE RULE, OD-FC-136: before it every seat drags free and nothing runs; it is adopted inside that advance's one commit, §3.1.38), so there the spatial arm is the default arm and the aspatial arm the degraded one. MEASURED at master `81f1cd963` (CONFIRMED by read): the one explicit canon entitlement today is the canonize call's own (`campaignWorldPulseSlice.js :: canonizeCampaignWorldSpatial`: `if (state.auth?.tier !== 'premium') return { ok: false, reason: 'not_entitled' };`), and only a premium or staff account can create a campaign (`campaignSlice.js :: createCampaign`: `const canCreate = current.auth?.tier === 'premium' || staffUnlocksPaidFeatures(current.auth?.role);`) — so H22's birth canon crosses no paying boundary that exists today; it reaches staff, and any lower tier the owner later admits to campaigns, as geography only. What H22 does move is BEHAVIOUR: the birth canon lights every canon-gated lane from the first week (the map document's own "behaviour switch"; its population-9.3-1 and plague-10.2-3), a declared new-world shift carried to the owner in §7 OD-FC-62 beside the born campaign's switches. The spine's requirement 17 carries the same text.

18. **THE TUNING-EVIDENCE LAW.** A band row carries its measured spread (seed
    family, preset, window) or the word UNMEASURED; the owner signs measured
    rows only; "absent means zero" needs a dormancy reason written down. (The
    board counts 255 tuning tables; the chain-first amendment had to add this
    outside the constitution.)

19. **THE FELT-PROOF LAW.** A mechanism is DONE when its DM-felt sentence fires
    in a lit preset on a seeded world and an experience read scores it — not
    when its fixture pin is green. Every wave names that sentence in its
    charter; a wave with no sentence is instrumentation and says so. (The
    chain-first rule, constitutionalised; seven families carry mechanisms with
    no DM sentence anywhere.)
    The felt sentence is an OUTCOME, and its coherence shows on scrutiny
    (requirement 16, WHAT IS SHOWN).

    **THE LEGIBILITY PIN AND C0.5** (§7 OD-FC-54; by delegation, and THE OWNER'S TO VETO). No chain's experience read can pass this law's legibility half (the owner's restatement under requirement 16: "completely legible and coherence when scrutinized") without a scrutiny surface, so CW-2x (the cause walk) and the Why panel are re-sequenced to C0.5 THE CAUSE WALK, right after C0 (§6). The walk is ONE generic reader over requirement 20's `causedBy` links (no per-system wiring); the Why panel renders it two hops deep by default. THE LEGIBILITY PIN: every pushed Herald line resolves to at least one hop in the walk (no orphan line) — a test inside every chain's done gate from C0.5 on. The re-sequencing re-orders a program the owner sequenced under FP-40 and is recorded as THE OWNER'S TO VETO.

20. **THE PROVENANCE-ADOPTION LAW.** Requirement 7's "walks backward link by
    named link" names its seam: every child receipt of a known outcome
    populates `causedBy` at its mint (the provenanceKernel road). (ARCH_CW's SC-6
    pre-pin is cited by zero volumes; the tree has two engine writers of
    `causedBy`, unchanged since August; CW-3's chain-depth envelope reds by
    design.)

21. **THE COUPLING-IDENTITY AND VOCABULARY LAW.** (a) Each program's modules
    belong to ONE of the seven coupling layers, declared once (ES→INFO, EC→TRADE,
    WC→WAR, HB→INFO, INT stays INTERIOR), or declare themselves a CARRIER across
    named layers (WY across TRADE/POP/WAR; EP across all, as a clock) — a
    declared multi-layer carrier is lawful, an undeclared one is not. Five programs declare here: PL (the polity, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PL.md`) a CARRIER across GRAMMAR · WAR · INTERIOR · TRADE · INFO (PL §9's own line); MN (the menace, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_MN.md`) a CARRIER across WAR · POPULATIONS · TRADE · INFO (MN §9's own line); RN (the renown, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_RN.md`) a CARRIER across INFO · INTERIOR · FAITH · GRAMMAR (the rumor road and the operative's fame; a court's regard derived from its people of power and the person lens out of the seat books; the priest's lean in the faith contest; the envoy's lean at `reserveFor`, ⛔ RN-S4, slot C1); CH (the champions, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_CH.md`) a CARRIER across all seven — FAITH · WAR · GRAMMAR · INFO · POPULATIONS · TRADE · INTERIOR — one domain seam each (CH §6: faith · war · diplomacy · information · health · trade · order; `wildland` rides MN's siege verdict, WAR); PT (the party, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PT.md`) a CARRIER across INFO · INTERIOR · GRAMMAR (the deed's telling and the truth that chases it; the gate, the bans and the summons-to-bounty ladder at a court; requests, offers and pledges between the party and courts). RN, CH and PT carry no coupling line of their own: their three declarations are this fold's reading of each volume's seam tables (RN §5.4/§10, CH §6, PT §7.3/§14) — PLAUSIBLE until each volume states its own line. Each program's prefix — `PL`, `MN`, `RN`, `CH`, `PT` — is chartered in the same commit as its first coupling-registry row (21c); the prefix `PT` names the volume, never the party's actor ref; (b) pair ids are closed at CPL-1..21 with a declared
    extension family for within-layer and new-program rows (the registry's
    CPL-22/23 are re-anchored, §3.3); (c) a new volume prefix is chartered in
    the same commit as its first registry row; (d) a closed ladder is minted
    into the corpus VOCABULARY REGISTER (§5) with a homonym check — one word,
    one meaning per surface, the J-WR-10-B borrow census generalised.

22. **THE ENGINE-STATE LIFECYCLE AND ERRATUM LAW.** (a) ARCH_SP's L4
    lifecycle-paths clause (create / read / persist / regenerate / undo /
    import / migrate / dark-after-lit / death / veil) binds every engine-written
    state, not only DM edits (requirement 14b); (b) an erratum reported against
    the constitution or a volume lands as a dated bracket in the same commit as
    the ruling that makes it true — "a bug to report" is not a disposition
    (three constitution errata stood unapplied for ten weeks: the alignment
    read, the errand ledger's home, the posture names); (c) requirement 11
    points at the owner's EVIDENCE LAW (2026-10-02/03, "complete, not cryptic";
    pillar 10) and the voice program ("actions, not potential") as binding on
    every phrased kind — COUPLINGS §9's contract sentences and §8 of every
    family are re-authored at build under it.

23. **THE RETENTION-HORIZON LAW.** Every ring (pulseHistory 80, wizardNews
    240, turningPoints 24, the provenance edge cap) declares its horizon in
    INTERVAL_WEEKS at a measured event rate, and every advance unit declares
    what it evicts: a `one_year` advance appends 52 records into the 80-ring
    before collapsing to one, so the ring nets to 29 (findings EP §6 S1, read
    from `historyRingCollapse.test.js:150-159`) — a year-cadence DM's receipt
    and replay horizon is about 29 advances, not 80, and requirement 7's cause
    walk ages out 2.7× faster at that clock. The ring policy itself (collapse
    before eviction, or raise the cap) is ruled by delegation (§7 OD-FC-33).

24. **THE HEARING WEEK** (the owner, 2026-10-16, 18:5x EDT, verbatim: "When it
    comes to conflict, spying, treatise, etc, anything that involves one
    settlement with another, the height of that engagement, regardless of where
    it is at, is paused at a week so that the user can always fix them however
    they want. … two antagonistic armies meet in a road. Their result is not
    instant and scrapped as part of advance time in the same week where the DM
    can't do anything. Instead, they pause at the 'start of battle' and the DM
    is given choice to optionally weight in on who is to win. Same with NPCs in
    other settlements or with other settlement's NPCs for any act of foreign
    policy. An envoy must stay a week in a settlement before the decision is
    made. A spy must stay a week as well, and anything related." — and the
    three clarifications: "this only applies to week by week advancements.
    months seasons and years go by automatically." · "The simulator is gated
    for premium" · "some options aren't just settled in a week. For example a
    siege. a siege takes a long time and either side may get allies to turn the
    tide over the course of weeks. still the DM can decide who wins during any
    one of those weeks forcing the hand.")
    (a) Every ENGAGEMENT between two settlements — an act of one court, army,
    envoy, spy, caravan, column, legate, house, or any NPC of one settlement
    upon another settlement or its NPCs — is a row in ONE ENGAGEMENT REGISTRY,
    seeded from the census of every live inter-settlement act (§3.4) and CLOSED
    once lit (an unregistered kind may not resolve; registration is the only
    door) (kind · owning program · its CLIMAX tick · its
    verdict vocabulary · whether it is a CLIMAX kind, settled in one hearing, or
    a STANDING kind that holds for weeks); its reach beyond two settlements is THE WIDENED
    REGISTRY, below. (b) THE DOCKET EXISTS BUT THE PAUSE CURSOR CANNOT HOST A
    HEARING: the landed majors docket (`decisionTier.js`, eleven majors;
    `advanceInterval.js:601`; J-D7's "ONE gathered adjudication screen" and
    "durable HELD DOCKET") is the SCREEN and the ledger idiom SP-9 generalises,
    but its paused cursor re-runs the same parked tick and refuses advances and
    edits while parked (`advanceInterval.js:327-340`,
    `campaignWorldPulseSlice.js:450-459`, `settlementPendingEdits.js:136-142`),
    so a hearing NEVER parks the cursor. At a one-week advance — the DM's
    chosen unit `one_week`, not a tick labelled `one_week` inside a longer
    advance — the engine never resolves a climax in the tick it arrives: the
    resolver writes the engagement to the HELD DOCKET (a flag-gated ledger) with
    its default verdict, computed deterministically with its epoch, and the tick
    COMPLETES normally; the world is in its ordinary between-advance state, so
    the DM may advance, edit or seal; the DM may leave the hearing to the engine,
    TILT it (a bounded thumb for one side, slight · heavy — a declared bounded
    term on the decider's score where the decider draws no randomness, a draw
    bias where it does; each kind declares which) or give a VERDICT (the forced
    outcome; the word "decree" is the landed Decree Registry's and is not used);
    the NEXT advance resolves every docketed hearing FIRST, from the seal or the
    default, before anything else moves. A STANDING engagement (a siege, a
    blockade, an embargo, an occupation's contest, a long negotiation, a spy's
    stay) offers the same three choices EVERY week it stands, and the verdict
    forces the hand that week. The HEARING SEAL is its own Edit Mode seal kind,
    keyed by ENGAGEMENT id with the registry's verdict vocabulary, read by the
    resolver FIRST (before any feasibility, max-age or capitulation arm) in the
    resolver's own module — it is not a fork pin, so the pinnable-fork list in
    the frozen `pulseKernel.js:362` is untouched; the STRESSOR PIN of
    ODQ §128 is a different object (it holds a stressor; the hearing seal decides
    a week's verdict) and both stand. (c) A month, season or year advance runs every HEARING on the engine's
    default without stopping — the owner's "months seasons and years go by
    automatically" governs hearings only; the landed majors pause and its DM
    switch (`advanceAutoResolve`, realm directive 7) are UNTOUCHED (that
    pause is the DM's option, and its majors include acts that are not between
    two settlements); each hearing still mints its docket record for one tick and
    leaves its receipt ("resolved by the engine's default verdict"), so the cause
    walk and the Herald see the same world either way. When the flag is dark or
    the world regenerates, docketed hearings are not read: a dark world resolves
    every climax in its tick as today, and a regeneration re-derives the docket
    from the world (it is derived state, dropped and re-minted, requirement 22a).
    (d) The seal is a
    typed DM edit (requirement 14; L10's one door) whose seal EVENT is minted
    with its own id and named as the verdict's parent in `causedBy` (the field
    holds parent ids), replayed exactly as an order in the world, surviving every lifecycle path (requirement 22a). (e) The
    simulator is PREMIUM: the hearing, the tilt and the verdict are premium
    surfaces (U16's gate), with no free-tier arm to design. (f) Dark-identical
    behind `hearingWeekEnabled`; lit, every climax resolves one tick later than
    today — a declared same-seed shift signed once at lighting. (g) Every
    engagement kind's pending card is its FIRST-VISIBLE sign (requirement 16)
    and is written under the Evidence Law: who, upon whom, where, the default
    verdict, and nothing about what the DM might do; at a month-or-longer
    advance the RECEIPT ("resolved by the engine's default verdict") is the sign.

    **THE OWNER'S RESTATEMENT** (2026-10-17), verbatim: "Also just to make sure that
    the simulator has this right as well for any hearing on a week by week basis
    that the dungeon, master or user has not made a decision on is then left to
    the RnG of the simulator to the side. Hearings only happen on the week.
    Which means something that was held up three weeks ago when we decided to
    advance by a month is not what we’re looking at." — (b) and (c) bind EVERY
    hearing kind, the party's and the champions' included
    (`DESIGN_FP_PARTY_BRIEF.md` §6 withdraws its earlier queue exception): a
    hearing is minted only at the DM's one-week advance and lives that one week,
    the next advance resolves it first from the DM's answer or the default, and
    nothing is held over an advance. A party hearing's default is that nothing
    has happened to the party (the party's fate is never the engine's); the RNG
    decides only the other actor's course. The landed majors pause, the DM's own
    switch, is a different object and stays as (c) rules.

    **THE WIDENED REGISTRY** (§7 OD-FC-50, by delegation on OD-MN-8's precedent, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_MN.md` §11–§12; vetoable). The owner's law speaks of "one settlement with another"; its widening to a menace, a person and the party is RULED BY DELEGATION on OD-MN-8's precedent, vetoable, and carried to the owner as a VISIBLE row (VISIBLE ROW 1 of ODQ §934.93). (a)'s registry also holds the engagements of: THE MENACE — an engagement between a settlement (or its army) and a MENACE, the DM-named outside force: `menace_siege` (STANDING, `holds · falls · relieved · lifted`; never `menace_strikes`, the name it replaced) and `menace_met` (CLIMAX); a menace's raiding is its stressor's ordinary life, not a hearing. THE PARTY (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PT.md` §13: FIFTEEN kinds — `party_request · party_offer · party_summons · party_warrant · party_bounty · party_hunted · party_ambush · party_visit` STANDING; `party_pledge_due · party_slander · party_gate · party_banned · backing_voided · backing_repudiated` CLIMAX, and `party_voiced` CLIMAX, below; every party kind's default "nothing has happened to the party yet", the RNG deciding only the other actor's course). THE CHAMPIONS (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_CH.md` §18, OD-CH-3: nine kinds between a court and a person or two persons in a town — `retainer_offer · paragon_resigns · champion_duel · person_at_the_gate · person_banned · banished_found · paragon_disgraced · polarity_turn · never_line_tested`, all CLIMAX). The polity's renamed `polity_*` kinds (§3.4). THE BODIES' kinds — registry rows like any other (§3.4), none a party kind; where a body meets the party, the card is PT's road arm with requirement 24's party default ("nothing has happened to the party yet"). THE OPPORTUNITY's and THE FISCAL SEAT's kinds (below). (b) and (c) bind every one of them exactly: a hearing only at the DM's one-week advance, nothing held over an advance, month/season/year advances on defaults — and an ADVANCE BY N YEARS (OD-FC-51) runs its whole span on defaults.

    **THE REACH, COUNTED FIRST** (§7 OD-FC-76; by delegation, vetoable). The widening to the party, champion, menace and body kinds STANDS. It rests on the owner's own words: "Hearings only happen on the week." (2026-10-17) and "and remember every engagement, skirmish, terrorizing a town, is a hearing." (2026-10-18 ~19:3x EDT, verbatim on the kit's BOARD). The independent panel's COUNT-FIRST is C0's FIRST MEASUREMENT: a wartime week's docket is counted on three seeds, every STANDING kind's weekly re-mint included, before any widened family lights. Each family LIGHTS only as that count allows, and C0's experience read decides (OD-FC-19's idiom). The docket ranks its cards by THE STAKES-RANKING FUNCTION (§7 OD-FC-76; DRAFT, UNMEASURED). The push budget's surface (requirement 12, above) shows the count, the top three by stakes and the two acts, and every card still exists.

    **THE ACTOR CONVENTION** (§3.3.12; OD-FC-57; by delegation, vetoable). Every such kind's parties use the one actor-ref convention `{kind, id?}` with `kind ∈ settlement · person · party · menace · corps · body` — no seventh actor kind (THE INTERIOR HEARING, below); the docket's `targetSaveId` is always a real settlement (the anchor) and a non-settlement party rides a `subject` dial (the MN convention). An ACTOR is a party that ACTS on a card: by its own choice (a settlement's court, a person, a corps — a corps' weekly choice is its own, CH §16b, and its key is RN's `u:<unitKey>`), by the table's (the party), or by the DM's authored course standing in for a mind (a menace: it has no mind, MN6, and still acts, because its course chooses for it — MN1, MN12). A SITE has no course and no choice: it is a WHERE (`ENGAGEMENT_WHERE_KINDS`) and a SUBJECT, never an actor (ST4). The test is whether the party acts on the card, not whether it has a mind, so the menace and the site part for a stated reason. A BODY is a conclave, a gang or a beast that moves on the land (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_BD.md` §2.1): ONE kind, with `bodyKind ∈ conclave · gang · beast` a FIELD on the ref (`{ kind: 'body', bodyKind, id }`), as a person's archetype is. It acts on a card by its own weekly choice (BD §4), so it passes the test above.

    **THE ANCHOR AND THE WHERE** (by delegation, vetoable) — two separate fields: the ANCHOR is the docket's key (`targetSaveId`) and the WHERE is the place the map draws. THE ANCHOR: the docket's `targetSaveId` is the actor's real settlement; where the actor stands in no settlement the kind names its anchor — `party_hunted` anchors on the court that set the bounty; a paragon pressing `party_visit` while on a leg anchors on its leg's origin; a menace's kinds anchor on the pressed town; no kind ever anchors on the party, which has no settlement of its own. THE WHERE: every kind names the place the map can name (the map's ENC-HL-13 and H16): §3.4's WHERE table, over the closed list `settlement · road · country · site · border_segment`; PT §13's cells are of record for the party's kinds: `party_hunted` on `road(court, first hop toward the party's last-heard place)`; `party_ambush` on `road(actor's town, the party's town)` when the two towns are neighbours, else `country(actor's town)`; a travelling paragon's `party_visit` at the `settlement` where it stands, else its leg's origin (§3.4's WHERE table).

    **WHOSE WORDS.** The registry's CLOSURE — which kinds exist, their owners, and that an unregistered kind cannot resolve — is this fold's; each row's verdict words, default, tilt and WHERE are the OWNING volume's registry row, of record (§3.4).

    **`party_voiced` — THE HEARING COMES FIRST.** A court's or a renowned person's public praise or condemnation of the party is an act of the world toward the party (the owner's warrant, OD-FC-50: "Everything with the party is a hearing nothing is ever decided for them"), so PT registers one more CLIMAX kind, `party_voiced` (verdicts `spoken · withheld`; default `spoken` — it decides nothing FOR the party; the DM may strike or amend it in its week). RN §5.5's crossing — a COURT (any), or a PERSON or CORPS of `regional` fame or wider, would publicly praise or condemn the party — mints nothing; the person stage hands the act to PT's engagement pass, which opens the `party_voiced` card; RN's writer mints the `voiced_regard` deed only when that hearing resolves `spoken`, and `withheld` mints nothing — so no hearer can hear a voice before the DM's week. RN owns no kind; a voice about anyone but the party stays regard dynamics, never a hearing (§3.4). The default `spoken` AGREES with the owner's restatement above ("A party hearing's default is that nothing has happened to the party … the RNG decides only the other actor's course"): speaking is the other actor's course, nothing is decided for the party, and what moves is only what hearers believe, as with `party_slander`. A court is heard by its standing, a person or a corps by its fame; a court's `voiced_regard` deed is a settlement doer's deed and travels as a TALE (RN1: no court has fame), moving hearers' regard of the party through credibility and the tale, as `party_slander` does; its court arm moves each hearer's regard by the hearer's relation to the voicing court (RN §5.5; §3.4 `party_voiced`; PT §7.3).

    **THE CLOCKED CARD** (BD §12's CLOCKED CARD and §9.10; by delegation, vetoable). An engagement with a clock (BD §12's CLOCKED kinds: the ransom race, a guild war, a pending sponsorship, a hunt taken, an alms request) keeps ONE card on the docket EVERY week of its clock. It is STANDING until its climax week — in every week before it the card is AVAILABLE with its weekly line and its current default (the owner, 2026-10-18 18:55 EDT: "the hearing is still avaialble to the DM until its automatically resolved.") — and its ONE CLIMAX week is its automatic resolution at the clock's end, where (b) binds; an EARLY act in any standing week — the DM's decision, or a line the DM leaves or tilts that resolves the engagement that week (a court's payment, a hunt's victory) — CLOSES it that week, and that week is then its climax. The DM's act is "decide", never "decree" ((b)'s word rule), and in every standing week the verdict forces the hand that week ((b); the owner's 10-16 clarification: "still the DM can decide who wins during any one of those weeks forcing the hand."). (b) and (c) bind it exactly: nothing queues; a month-or-longer advance resolves each week on its default. ONE PUSH: a clocked card's standing-week lines are scrutiny lines and are never pushed; its CLOSE — at the clock's end or early — is the card's ONE pushable line, ranked by the budget like any CLIMAX card.

    **THE RANSOM RACE'S CLOSE** (§3.3.13; §3.4 `ransom`; §5, the ransom race's words; by delegation, vetoable). The owner, 2026-10-18 18:54 EDT: "this also changes the ransom rules if they are taken by a gang or conclave. if the gang can survive with teh NPC for long, then they will have the ransom granted, if they are defeated, then the captive goes free." So SURVIVING THE CLOCK IS `ransom_granted`: the automatic resolution PAYS whether or not the court chose to pay, (b) forces the hand that week, and only the DM's own decision overrides it. During the clock the court may pay early (`ransom_granted` that week), hunt (a defeat first frees the captive, `captive_freed`) or wait. `captive_released` is no automatic outcome: it is only the body's own act, receipted, when its sustenance cannot hold the captive, or the DM's decision.

    **THE INTERIOR HEARING** (⭐ OD-FS-9, THE OWNER'S, VISIBLE — never a plain delegation; §7 OD-FC-88). (a)'s registry also holds ONE engagement INSIDE one town, THE FISCAL SEAT's `tax_refusal` (STANDING; WHERE `settlement`), on THE BODIES' `guild_war` precedent — a widening beyond "anything that involves one settlement with another" and beyond the widening to a menace, a person and the party (itself VISIBLE ROW 1 of ODQ §934.93); THE INTERIOR ACTOR RULE: exactly ONE party acts on the card, the town's SEAT (`{ kind: 'settlement', id }`); the commons are the card's CIRCUMSTANCE (POP-2's refusal rung; the hand on the `subject` dial), never an actor — no seventh actor kind is minted; only POP-2's target `hand` is a hearing (a seat act), its other targets keep POP-2's posture-coloured answer; the VETO ARM is whole: the seat answers in its tick by FS §10.1's default, the DM's levers being `set-levy` and the stressor pin; (b)'s "first" is met at the HEARING — the verdict is sealed first and reaches the NEXT week's mint — the levy is cut from next week — and no resolve-first stage is claimed for interior kinds.

    **THE OPPORTUNITY'S `rescue_attempt`** (CLIMAX; `caught · freed · refused`; WHERE `settlement`, the VENUE town): a `person` (the rescuer — an errand traveller or a paragon) upon the JAILER's `settlement` (`jailerOf`), the captive the card's subject, never an actor; never a party kind (OP13); where a `ransom` race stands the same week THE RESCUE RESOLVES FIRST (§3.4's rows; §7 OD-FC-93).

    **THE KINDS NOT MINTED.** THE MOVEMENT GRAMMAR and THE WAY OF WAR mint NO kind — a verb is never a hearing (MG6; its contacts are the kinds their owners registered), and a way only adds one Why line to the war kinds' cards (WS §8); THE PATRON DEITY mints none (`person_at_the_gate` gains the reason words `patron_creed` · `suppressed_creed`, §3.4).

    SP-9's charter carries the two interior and rescue kinds (dated brackets in `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_SP.md`); the registry's text of record is §3.4 here, whose recount holds NINETY-TWO kind names in EIGHTY-SEVEN live rows when each program kind takes its own row (EIGHTY-ONE as the volumes group them; §3.4 THE COUNT). The spine's requirement 24 carries this requirement's text (mirrored into `DESIGN_FP_SPINE.md` §1 at requirement 24).

A receipt of the form NAME:n cites the corpus as read at `00b648c9d` (§2); a row that a later ruling amended reads as amended, under its own number, so a cite of a row resolves to its final text.

**THE INBOX** — rows routed to a program's own next unit; under the never-deferred law each row IS its slot (the owner's law: there is never deferred work). The rows are grouped by the program that owns the unit, and each keeps its number, so a cite such as "the fold's §3.1 WC INBOX (14)" or "the map INBOX (j)" resolves here. A row already PLACED in its owner volume says so and asks nothing new; a row whose owner volume is outside the ledger tree CARRIES THE TEXT verbatim.
- **THE GRAMMAR (GR):** (1) `pact_refused`'s erasure by ensure (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PL.md` §16). (2) The hostile-pair NON-AGGRESSION occasion against a shared menace — MN keeps the damper and DROPS the occasion until GRAMMAR lands an admission change. (3) THE SIX-SITE SUCCESSION RECORD — the one record every seat change (a DM KILL/ASSIGN included) would write, the producer PL's fealty question lacks (§3.1.10). (4) The court-bond flavours `court_match` · `fosterage` · the parked envoy kind `hostage_exchange` (a persisted-vocabulary change, owner-visible) and the autonomy charter's term row `narrowed_sphere` (OD-PL-9, OD-PL-33). **KEEP YOUR PEACE** (BD's route; GR mints it): the "keep your peace" demand row fed by `peace_unkept` (the cause family that retired the compile's `brigandage_exported`, BD §2.6), its refusal a casus on the landed grievance ladder (cite, never a second ladder); and the "no hand in each other's hills" clause as ONE new term on GR's landed `peaceTermsCatalog.js :: TERM_CATALOG`. (5) THE `site_cession` TERM: ONE term row on `peaceTermsCatalog.js :: TERM_CATALOG` beside the trade-rights rows — CEDED in a peace, the seam executor calling THE SITES' one writer (ST §6.3; ST §7.4's GR row) — in GRAMMAR's commit. (6) COMMUTED DUES: the autonomy charter's commuted-dues term — ONE GRAMMAR term row; PL marks it DARK until carried (PL §3's compromises). (7) THE DEBTOR COURT'S RECEPTION LEAN (OP §15 GR / SP-D2): `ENVOY_RECEPTION_DECISIONS` leans `receive` for a creditor court's envoy — one bounded lean — placed in GR. (8) WS's C10 treaty appetite rides the ONE `reserve01` joint leaf as its third claimant (§3.1.24) — placed in GR (GR-2).
- **WAR CIRCULATION (WC):** (1) VASSAL GARRISONS — NEW CAPABILITY, out of PL (research R4 Q1; OD-PL-33), never built inside PL: a garrison held as a site is THE SITES' (§3.1.29), and a seat's force band is the slot §3.1.29 records. (2) The `CALL_IN_REQUEST` anchor widening for a menace, and the mapping of a raiding menace onto WC-12's contest (`suppression · worn_down · buy_off`; never `absorbed`) (OD-MN-19). THE TEST: a kind is an actor when it ACTS on the card — by its own choice (a court, a person, a corps, a body), by the table's (the party), or by the DM's authored course standing in for a mind (a menace: MN1, MN6, MN12). A site has no course and no choice: it is a WHERE kind (`ENGAGEMENT_WHERE_KINDS`, §3.4) and a SUBJECT (the `subject` dial), never an actor (ST4). THE POSTURE DOOR, said once: `POSTURE_ACTOR_KINDS` is SP-4a's ONE CLOSED ACTOR SET — master names it so ("THE CLOSED ACTOR SET (SP §8 seam contract 9)", `worldPulse/strategicPosture.js`) and `assertActor` throws on any other kind — and on master it reads `worldPulse/strategicPosture.js :: POSTURE_ACTOR_KINDS = Object.freeze(['settlement'])`, so the set is widened BY NAME to ADMIT the six refs, in the first wave that reads a non-settlement actor through the posture door; the door COMPUTES a posture for a kind only where that kind's owning volume names the read, and for a `menace` (its course is the DM's, MN6) or the `party` (its choices are the table's) it returns a stated refusal, never a number. THE KEYS: a corps' renown key is RN's UNIT key `u:<unitKey>`, holder and subject (the refused-pair rule binds `s:` and `c:` only and never applies to `u:`; `c:` stays the court body's prefix), and the deed's `doer` and `target` admit it (RN §2.1–§2.2); a named gang's key is RN's `g:<bodyId>` (a conclave keys its creed, `creed:<ref>`; a beast none). PT's exclusion-edge actor-ref widening has a `corps` arm; THE PASSAGE judges a RETAINED corps as a passage mover (PS1 (d), `PASSAGE_MOVER_KINDS`) and a FREE corps meets a town at CH's gate (`person_at_the_gate`'s corps arm; PS §5.5, OD-PS-34). THE HOLDER REF: an untied site holder is THE BODIES' `{ kind: 'body', bodyKind: 'gang' \| 'beast', id }`; a CONCLAVE holds as THE SITES' `faith` holder with a body ref; `cult` is never a holder kind and never villainy (the owner's word of 2026-10-18 17:27 EDT, verbatim: "remember that not all cults are evil. cults are simply small conclaves of religion."; §5 `cult` · `band`). THE BACKER'S ROLES: the convention is NEVER widened by qualifiers — a `backing` names its backer as `{kind:'settlement', id}` plus ONE closed field `backerRole`: `backerRole ∈ BACKER_ROLES` = `town_court` (the town's seat) · `town_power` (a power in the town, with `factionId`) · `liege_court` (the town's liege; `by.id` is the liege's seat) · `paramount_court` (the polity's paramount; `by.id` is the root) · `creed_clergy` (a faith's clergy there, with `creed`), so the crown and the liege are two values and `by.id` always names the backing court; RN §2.2 maps each to ONE regard key — `c:<by.id>` · `f:<by.id>:<factionId>` · `c:<by.id>` · `c:<by.id>` · `creed:<creed>`; with `polityEnabled` dark the two polity roles are not offered; all five values are FREE on master (0 src · 0 tests, `cure/D-FOLD-r2-probe/tokens-r2.out`). Every hearing's parties use the convention; the proposals queue's `targetSaveId` is always a real settlement (the anchor) for every kind, and the actor ref — or a site — rides a `subject` dial (the MN convention); `RumorContent.partyIds` keeps its landed meaning (the involved SETTLEMENTS) and never holds an actor ref. SITES CONSIDERED: SP-9's held docket hosts THE SITES' kinds by registration like any other (`registerEngagementKind`) and §3.4's WHERE `site` row names the place; every other program considers sites BY CONSTRUCTION through the one reads (`homeDefense`, the embattlement term, `buildThreatByCid`, `scoreRoute`, `care01`, the faith contest's inputs, the witness plane — ST §7.1), with no per-decider wiring. §1 requirement 24 (THE ACTOR CONVENTION) and the spine's requirement 24 and SP-4 say the same. | SPINE SP-4a, SP-4, requirement 24; §1 requirement 24, THE ACTOR CONVENTION; MN §2; CH §11, §16b, §18, OD-CH-36; PT §2.1, §2.4, §13 (the edge's corps arm); RN §2.1–§2.2, R49, OD-RN-36, OD-RN-46; PS1, §5.5; BD §2.1, OD-BD-3; ST4, ST §2.1, §6.7, §7.1, §7.3, §7.4, OD-ST-43/44; OD-FC-57 |

NO ENGINE CUSTODY OF A PARAGON UNTIL ES's CUSTODY WAVE LANDS: no second custody road; no second meaning of `jailed` (`npcVerdictTable.js :: HOLDING_VERDICT`; no paragon hold by `verdictCause: 'jailed'`); no `paragon.held` field; the failure ladder STOPS AT DISGRACE; `banished_found` has NO `seized` (its verdicts `expelled · let_be`); the DEGRADED ARM is the DM's own hand, an Edit Mode act placing the paragon "held at X" as authored whereabouts, with no engine hold; the champion is NOT YET a consumer of `abandoned_to_captivity`; CH keeps no landed custody arm as live design (OD-CH-11's narrow arm — a bound paragon on a court errand held by the landed `foreignGuestHold` — stands for no engine hold) and writes its ransom-row consumption as "when ES's custody wave lands" (CH §0, §12.4, CH12). The landed roads hostage (`roads/state.js :: RansomRec`) holds ROSTER persons and the DM's ransom and rescue ride the landed `ransom-npc` / `rescue-npc` ops; neither is a paragon custody road. WHY THE LANDED HOLD CANNOT REACH A PARAGON (CONFIRMED by read at `81f1cd963`): `foreignGuestHold.js :: normalizeForeignGuestHold` returns null unless the row carries `id`, `npcId`, `errandId`, `encounterId`, `captorId` and `venueId` (`custodyWritten: false`), and its one production opener, `envoyInterceptionStage.js :: holdInterruptedEnvoy`, holds the bearer of an INTERCEPTED ENVOY-LEDGER ERRAND ROW, keying the hold on `errand.npcId` and reading no traveller kind — but no paragon can stand on such a row (`envoyErrandRecords.js :: normalizeErrand` returns null without a peace offer; `envoyErrandVocabulary.js :: ENVOY_PURPOSES = Object.freeze(['sue', 'self_parlay'])`), so until the owner's errand-row slot lands a bound paragon goes abroad with its court's army or column, or where the DM sends it. THE PAIRING: that errand-row slot and ES-2b land together, or the row slot carries a named paragon exclusion at the interception stage — never the row slot alone (the §3.1 SP-D INBOX). THE RANSOM RACE (BD §9.10; the owner's word of 2026-10-18 18:54 EDT, verbatim: "this also changes the ransom rules if they are taken by a gang or conclave. if the gang can survive with teh NPC for long, then they will have the ransom granted, if they are defeated, then the captive goes free."): the BODY's side of the custody slot, changing nothing on the custody side, and DARK until ES's custody wave lands. SURVIVING THE CLOCK = `ransom_granted`: the automatic resolution PAYS — the court's treasury into the body's sustenance through the one treasury writer (W-COIN; until it lights, a band step of the court's funding), a deed on the body's ledger — whether or not the court chose to; requirement 24 (b) forces the hand that week and only the DM's own decision overrides it. The court's choices DURING the clock are `pay · hunt · wait` ("hunt" the court's act, its stance `hunted` and `hunt_taken`; "wait" requirement 24's leave): an early payment closes it `ransom_granted` (a `pay` in any week ends the race that week); a hunt that defeats the body first closes it `captive_freed` — the body DEFEATED first (a hunt's `snuffed_out` or `driven_off`, a rival body, starvation), unpaid, the freer's deed. The landed `HOME_ANSWERS` `pay · refuse · abandon` stay the court-to-court leaf's (`ransomChoices.js :: HOME_ANSWERS`); `refuse` and `abandon` only DELAY — the clock's end still pays, and `abandon` still marks the landed grievance `ABANDONMENT_GRIEVANCE_KIND` (`'abandoned_to_captivity'`). `captive_released` is NOT an automatic outcome: it is only the body's own act when its sustenance cannot hold the captive (it lets go early, receipted — the captor's landed `release`: `worldPulse/ransomChoices.js :: CAPTOR_CHOICES` "`Object.freeze(['hold', 'ransom', 'release'])`", re-read at master) or the DM's decision; notoriety only. WHO TAKES: a gang, or a HOSTILE (`curbed` or `outlawed`) flock, its god's axes a WEIGHT, never an absolute — an evil or warlike god's flock readily, a good one rarely and only in extremity (starving, hunted), a peaceable flock never (the owner's 19:3x word lists conclaves among the bodies that take "supplies and bodies", and the owner's 18:54 word names a conclave among the captors). The ENGINE NEVER HARMS THE CAPTIVE: the captive's fate past release is the DM's. The card is CLOCKED (§1 requirement 24, THE CLOCKED CARD), its close per THE RANSOM RACE'S CLOSE | §3.4 `ransom`, `envoy_held`, `banished_found`; §2's CH row; OD-FC-48, OD-FC-60; CH §0, §4, §12.4, CH12, OD-CH-11; BD §9.10, BD27, §12, S29, OD-BD-40; RN §4.6; PR §8.2; ES (the custody wave row); WR-7d; §1 requirement 24, THE CLOCKED CARD and THE RANSOM RACE'S CLOSE; §5 the ransom race's words; the §3.1 SP-D INBOX |
| 3.3.14 | **THE SEA** (the owner's question of 2026-10-18, verbatim: "Regarding weather and Sea route isn't this already in the books? Because I swear I talked about it like this already.") — a sea design without a naval program ("a ship = a column's or army's CARRIAGE (WC's union) … a sea fight = two columns meeting on a lane … storms = the calamity bucket … blockade = W11") contradicts a landed, lit program the owner commissioned: `docs/DESIGN_NAVY.md` (its header: "owner-commissioned: navies convoy own/allied armies on water routes; hostile navies meeting = a sea battle with LAND-PARITY semantics (loser retreats to home port); an army at sea shares its convoy's fate"); `navalKernel.js` ("SEA BATTLES: two HOSTILE navies whose paths share a SEA EDGE collide there"; "a navy blockading a hostile port MINTS A SIEGE"); `simulationRules.js :: WAVES` (`navalEnabled: true`, the W-R2-LIGHT owner ruling). **Where piracy is live, and who watches a harbour.** | THE SEA IS IN THE BOOKS, AND FP MINTS NO SEA LAW (by delegation, vetoable; FP's sea rows sit ON the landed carriers and never replace them): (a) lanes, ports and storm seasons are TRADE M8's — the augmented routing graph for a trip involving a port (`distanceRead.js :: routingAdjacency`), port eligibility by `distanceRead.js :: isPort` (geography AND institutions, the host document's law 16), storms by `seaLanes.js :: STORM_SEASON_COST` and `stormSeasonFactor` (slow, never sever); (b) NAVIES, CONVOYS, SEA BATTLES AND BLOCKADE-AS-SIEGE are W-NAVY's (`navalLayer.js`, `navalKernel.js`; `armyTransit.js :: ARMY_ROLES` `convoy · blockade`; the sibling `navalTransit` ledger) — a ship is a navy's CONVOY, never WC's union, and a sea fight is a battle between NAVIES, never two columns; W-NAVY is lit in the three world-alive presets and the lit default on a canonized realm, and a PLAIN-born campaign lacks `navalEnabled` until the birth door is lit (OD-FC-62); (c) PIRACY PARITY IS LIVE BY CONSTRUCTION for shipments — `spatial/navalLayer.js`, its Stage 5: "scoreRoute prices port-node embattlement into sea routes verbatim (pinned since M8) and banditryLoss fires on EVERY arriving shipment sea or land alike", and the module "adds NO danger math", naming a sea loss "piracy" only; blockade capture and storm delay ("priced not severed") are THE ROADS' D-6 sea hazards (`roads/seaRoads.js` S1–S2), and D-6 S3 ("piracy (S3 = banditry-by-construction)") is the ROADS-MISSION sea arm, which lights with `seaRoadsEnabled` ("ABSENT from DEFAULT_SIMULATION_RULES", lit in no preset; read at master `81f1cd963`); (d) a LANDING is a convoy's arrival or an army's debark at a port, and it is THE PASSAGE's harbour crossing (PS2 (ii): open water is nobody's territory and asks nowhere; a node entered by sea and left by land is a landing at B's harbour, asked at B's seat; PS §6.2), where THE SITES' `harbour_works` stands (only on an eligible port, conferring none and conferring no watch); a harbour's WATCH is its port's W-NAVY navy (PR OD-PR-37: "a harbour's watch is its W-NAVY navy (no slot of PR's)"), and a landing is always witnessed, because B's own seat is on the path ("no quiet landing exists", PS's crossing WHERE); (e) a storm on a lane is the landed storm law (M8's cost, D-6's S2 delay, never capture) — a lane holds no calamity record, and a storm that strikes a PORT TOWN is the calamity bucket THERE, by that settlement's own stressor; (f) islands by the one physics; fishing is THE SITES' `resource` kind, its yield a slot on EC (the §3.1 EC INBOX (2)); (g) "W11" names no wave (0 hits on master); no FP volume mints a sea fight, a sea law or a naval patrol damper (`navalLayer.js`: "no naval-patrol damper"; PR15: water carries no patrol fact) | MN §0, §6, OD-MN-38; PR §0.3, PR15, OD-PR-37; PS2, PS8, §6.2; CH §5, OD-CH-37; ST §2.2 `harbour_works`; TRADE M8; `docs/DESIGN_NAVY.md` (W-NAVY) |
| 3.3.15 | **THE BANNER — patrols in a kingdom** (the owner's word of 2026-10-18 ~19:2x EDT, verbatim: "Effectively, every patrol in a kingdom is working under the highest overlord banner, and every subsequent level until it reaches them inteh vassal chain with a home local font thet is their home settlement that they more or less take charge in and can with some flexibility operate in unity to cover the entirety of the kingdom region. This si also how we can express protection from the crown. so it blurs the line a bit between territories.") — three volumes held three readings of a patrol beyond its own land (PR: the ally ladder at every hold; PS: "members stand as allies"; PL: no rider row) | RULED (by delegation, vetoable): ONE HOME — the settlement that raises, pays and houses a patrol has first call on its own roads; its LOYALTY STACK is PL's chain read rung by rung (`polityOf`'s path with `crownReachAt` at each rung, cut at a suspended link), DERIVED and NEVER STORED (PR §10.3 (a); PL §4.7 F.1). ONE SHARE LAW — the liege's share of a member's riders is the same monotone band as its share of the member's force and decisions, never a second share (PL §4.7 F.2). THE LINE BLURS BY THE HOLD — the crown's net is called on the crown's OWN read of its hold, and each crossing's right on the HOLDER's own belief (THE PASSAGE's read): under a hold the holder believes `staunch` or `unbroken`, a same-kingdom patrol rides a member's roads BY RIGHT OF THE BANNER (THE PASSAGE's permit source `banner`, no tolerance spent; OD-PS-45) and coverage composes across members' borders under the one per-stretch cap; under a weaker hold each lord keeps his roads and a neighbour-lord's riders take the ally size tolerance; at a break the crown's riders are a FOREIGN UNIT in a rebel's land, under THE PASSAGE's rules (PR §10.3; PS §5.5, §14). PROTECTION IS A DEED, and its text of record is PL §4.7 F.4 (the owning volume wins): an occasion opens only when a member answers its liege's summons `answered` WITH ITS RIDERS while the crown's OWN read of its hold is `staunch` or `unbroken` — "Below `staunch` no occasion opens" (a weak crown promises no cover, and its `P` is not drained by a cover it never offered); the outcome is ONE row on the member's own sworn edge, read at the SEASON'S END from THE BANNER's season ledger — PR's per-member seasonal tally `weeksCovered` (the weeks in which a banner rider not the member's own stood placed on at least one of its road halves; reset at the season; PR's declared one-field addition) — `protection_kept` when the tally is above zero, `protection_failed` when it is zero, never from the one-week `lived` rows; lit only where THE PATROLS are and PL-5's summons exists (WC-5). The placement is the deed; the count of riders never enters ("came to its aid" is never a number). On an incursion the OUTCOME is the crossing's HARM whatever the answer was (`protection_kept` when the member suffered no incursion harm, `protection_failed` when it did) and the ANSWER moves trust; a lender's credit is ONE `service_answered` row a year on PL's own episode `riders_lent:<rootId>:<yearStartTick>` (PL §4.7 F.4, F.6). THE SUMMONS: PL §4.1 STANDS (under `unbroken` the crown may summon every member DIRECTLY), and the banner never skips a lord's ROW — the mid-lord's row is written either way (a credit when it lets its sub-vassal's riders go; a resentment patch when the crown rode past it unasked); under every weaker hold the call goes rung by rung. THE NET'S FLOOR: the net never lends zero at a strong hold — under a hold of `staunch` or better a member lends `max(1, floor(n × share01 × hold01))` riders when it keeps at least the minimum itself (`NET_HOME_MIN`, DRAFT); a SUB-VASSAL lends through its LIEGE's line (the chain, F.1), never at the crown's own share, and PL F.2's sum is recomputed so. THE SWEEP'S PRICE: F.6 (b) prices the crown's sweep of a member's roads as PL's RESENTMENT PATCH, never `riders_quartered` wear, and it fires ONLY ON THE LORD'S OWN NEED — when the crown's riders sweep a member's roads UNASKED for a need the member's own riders could have met; never on a fellow member's riders riding by right (F.3), never on a threat beyond the lord's reach; PL F.6 (b)(i) reads `bannerCoverOf(...).swept`, the weeks of the season in which a rider of the crown's OWN power stood on the member's road halves, beside `weeks` (THE BANNER's season ledger `weeksCovered`). NO NEW REGISTRY KIND: the banner rides `call_in_answer`'s one input line and THE PASSAGE's existing cards; `banner` is a prose word and one permit-source value (§5). FIRST VISIBLE (requirement 16): a patrol turned back at a border it rode freely last season | PR §2.7, §10.3, PR11, §10.1 (c), OD-PR-50 (the season ledger); PL §4.1, §4.3 (3), §4.7 F.1–F.6, OD-PL-54 · OD-PL-56; PS §5.5, §7.5, §14, OD-PS-45; §5's PR rows; OD-FC-75 |

Legend: LIT = the flag is in at least one shipped preset (the default preset is `realistic_regional`); DARK = built, flag minted or inert, lit nowhere; UNBUILT = no module; SEAM = a leaf with no caller. "Felt" = a DM sentence exists in the lit presets. For an earlier program the receipts are §2 of its family's read in the corpus read's kit (`settlement-engine-kits/fp-coherence-2fc13d59-2026-10-16/findings/`). For a volume of the FP final architecture program the Program cell carries the text's own status, BUILT, DARK names the landed substrate it rests on, UNBUILT names its own waves, and the volume itself is of record for its counts (flags, kinds, shapes, waves). Every such volume was reviewed adversarially to zero BLOCKING; the completeness critic and its wiring census (OD-FC-79) — the last check before "final for implementation" — ran over PL · MN · RN · CH · PT · PS · ST · PR · BD and this fold and closed on substance; what review left is parallel-cure drift between volumes, consolidated into one bracket-free packet per wave at dispatch (the review record is §9). The landed volumes keep their dated brackets under the insertions-only law — the inputs of those dispatch packets — while each of the program's fourteen volumes is one CONSOLIDATED text, its row saying when (THE REALM'S PAST was born consolidated, with no bracketed text of record).

**The one lit chain** (stated per preset from the executed census): in the DEFAULT preset `realistic_regional` a DM feels a pact proposed, valued by the responder, signed or declined, kept or frayed; and a patron's fall — which is VACUOUS on every born world because the birth preset seats no deity (FAITH:10, FPQ-62; OD-FC-134 decides an offer the DM confirms, unbuilt). Renewal, conversion, succession at an heir and mediation are lit only in `dramatic_campaign` and `full_simulation`; believed scarcity and conditions colour pacts only in the three belief presets, never the default (the default preset becomes the BELIEF preset once C0 lights, omniscience kept as a switch: OD-FC-62). Everything else in the corpus — the thirty volumes read at `00b648c9d` and every volume since — is a flag away or a build away.

**The default preset holds only on a world that carries its switches.** A PLAIN-born campaign today is named `realistic_regional` yet runs NONE of the twenty-three switches that preset lights (`pactFormationEnabled`, `faithUnseatingEnabled` and `navalEnabled` among them, so a plain-born world runs no naval layer either), because `NEW_CAMPAIGN_SIMULATION_PRESET_ID = null`; only a composer-born world (`composeInstantWorld.js :: applyTonePreset`) feels this chain. The cure is OD-EC-5's birth-door car (ODQ §934.90 addendum 1), EXECUTED and ruled by delegation in OD-FC-62: a born campaign carries the default preset's switches as well as its name, at the real count — +30 persisted keys, twenty-three of them switches, where the first yes named +7 — and the car's size is re-confirmed in chat at its unit. Until that car lands this chain is felt only on a composer-born world, and no "default preset" claim in this corpus is trusted for a plain-born world.

### 3.4 THE ENGAGEMENT REGISTRY (requirement 24; one closed list; the owning program registers each row in the wave that builds the kind; CLIMAX = one hearing, STANDING = a hearing every week, CLOCKED = a standing engagement with a clock whose close is its climax)

**WHOSE WORDS ARE OF RECORD** (by delegation, vetoable). The registry's CLOSURE is this fold's: which kinds exist, their owners, and that an unregistered kind cannot resolve. Each row's VERDICT WORDS, DEFAULT, TILT and WHERE are the OWNING VOLUME's registry row, of record — the owning program registers its row in the wave that builds the kind. The rows owned by a program volume are therefore REGENERATED from that volume's hearings table as it stands (each cell the volume's own words, a bare section cite in it written with the volume's code; the DEFAULT, the parties and the tilt stand in the volume's row, named in the last column), and where a fold cell and its owning volume differ, the volume wins and the fold's cell is the error. The rows whose owner is a LANDED volume (the war volume, WC, GRAMMAR, ES, INFO, WY, POP, WF, EC, the encounter stage) are of record HERE, each naming the program volumes that widen it.

| Kind | Owner (wave) | WHERE | Climax tick | Verdict vocabulary | Class | Of record |

THE PRECEDENCE — ONE CAPTIVE, TWO CARDS IN ONE WEEK: where a `rescue_attempt` (CLIMAX) and this race (STANDING — THE BODIES' race, or WR-7d's standing row for a foreign-guest hold) stand the same week, THE RESCUE RESOLVES FIRST (OP's stage before BD's race resolver and before WR-7d's standing ransom row); a DM SEAL ON THE RACE that week (`pay` · `hunt` · `wait`) WITHDRAWS the attempt as `refused` (the captive's own terms stand; receipt "the ransom was settled first"); a DM SEAL ON THE ATTEMPT BINDS THE RACE (`caught` → the race stands with two captives where the rescuer can be held — a PARAGON's `caught` writes no hold until the errand-row slot and ES-2b pair; `freed` → the race closes `captive_freed`); with neither sealed the ENGINE DEFAULT runs the attempt first and the race reads its closing; silence never decides between the two cards — the order does. **WHAT IS NOT A KIND, AND THE KINDS' NAMES.** `relief_answer` is NOT A KIND: the liege's decided answer (`sent · kept`, WC's words in the same meaning; the fealty record's `relief_sent` / `relief_kept` rows) is ONE answer for EVERY siege's relief — the war's `siege`, `menace_siege` and THE SITES' `site_siege` alike — an INPUT LINE on that siege's own card, one hearing per siege, never a second card; it is counted in the siege's verdict OR as a REINFORCE army, never both, and a menace siege's relief answers are INPUTS of `menace_siege` (MN §7). A SIEGE's relief answer is taken per candidate in the FIRST WEEK THAT CANDIDATE BELIEVES the siege (the hop delay sets it), never "week 1"; the INCURSION's liege line is taken at FIRST SIGHT on the liege's own belief inputs as they stand, and the first-believing-week rule binds siege relief only (PL §4.3, §7). `party_stopped` is no kind: `party_gate` is WIDENED to a road WHERE (CH's words unchanged). `patrol_order` is no kind: a member's riders are one input line on `call_in_answer`. `envoy_hearing` is no kind: it is merged into `pact_acceptance`. `person_held` is never minted. THE KINDS' NAMES are the renamed ones, read so everywhere: `crown_fallen` → `polity_fallen` (its `remnant` → `stand_by`; `legitimateRemnantOf` names a landed faction); `crown_claim` → `polity_claim`; `crown_war` → `polity_war`; `crown_arbitration` → `polity_arbitration`; the HEARING `fealty_broken` → `fealty_break` (the CASUS keeps `fealty_broken`, with its mirror `fealty_restored`); `menace_strikes` → `menace_siege` (the strike is calamity's word). RN, MG and WS mint no kind (a verb's contacts are the owners' kinds, MG6; WS adds one card line on the war kinds, OD-WS-7); OP's other acts ride the owners' kinds (OP §10.2); the CLASS column carries `CLOCKED` beside `STANDING` for an engagement with a clock (§1 requirement 24, THE CLOCKED CARD). THE REALM'S PAST mints no kind: the pencilled past is no engagement between two settlements and never stands on the docket, and the DM decides it with the pen before any week runs, through requirement 14's verbs (PA §15, OD-PA-19).

**THE COUNT** (regenerated by script from the fourteen program volumes' hearings tables and this registry's landed rows): the live registry holds NINETY-TWO kind names in EIGHTY-ONE rows — thirty-five kind names of the landed volumes in 30 rows (a pair row such as `lure_sprung` / `plant_taken` counts two kinds) and fifty-seven kind names of the program volumes in 51 rows (PL 9 · MN 2 · CH 9 · PT 15 in 9 rows · PS 4 · ST 5 · PR 2 · BD 9 · OP 1 · FS 1; RN, MG, WS and PA none). A volume's grouped row counts once as a row and once per kind: PT's six party kinds `party_request` · `party_offer` · `party_summons` · `party_warrant` · `party_bounty` · `party_visit` share one row and its two backing kinds another, so the same ninety-two kind names stand in 87 rows when each program kind takes its own row (the five landed pair rows counting once, as the old count did).

**THE WHERE OF EVERY KIND** (the map's ENC-HL-13: "Every engagement kind needs a place the map can name", and H16's border segment as a hearing card's "where"; the host ruling (3)). The closed list is `ENGAGEMENT_WHERE_KINDS` = `settlement · road · country · site · border_segment` (the identifier FREE on master; the values reuse landed words in their place meanings — `road` a registered homonym of the route grade). `site` is ADDITIVE when THE SITES' record lands (§3.1.28); `border_segment` is OFFERED only — the map's reading through H16, lit on the owner's word, never a host fact before it, so THE PASSAGE names the digest's `road (a, b)` from its first lit week. Each owning volume's WHERE cell is OF RECORD for its own kinds (the WHERE column above); this table is the index.

| WHERE | The kinds | What is named |
|---|---|---|
| `settlement` | `siege` · `pact_acceptance` · `mediation_offer` · `succession_reaffirmation` · `spy_at_target` · `lure_sprung` / `plant_taken` · `call_in_answer` · `contribution_dispatch` · `raid` (a body raider included) · `column_at_the_wall` · `legate_received` / `pilgrims_received` · `tribute_demand` / `trade_rights_demand` · `war_declaration` · `blockade` / `occupation_contest` · `negotiation` · `factor_parlay` · `peace_offer` · `coalition_join` · `treaty_breach` · `intervention` · `sovereignty_conveyance` · `envoy_held` · `ransom` (the captive's court) · `ratification` · `pact_renewal` · `embargo` · every PL kind · `menace_siege` · `menace_met` (at the column's arrival) · the nine CH kinds · `party_request` · `party_offer` · `party_summons` · `party_warrant` · `party_bounty` · `party_gate` · `party_visit` (HERE IN EVERY CASE — where the paragon stands, else its leg's origin) · `party_pledge_due` · `party_slander` · `party_banned` · `backing_voided` · `backing_repudiated` · `party_voiced` · `body_stance` · `alms_requested` · `body_bounty` · `hunt_taken` · `guild_war` · `sponsor_exposed` · `passage_request` (B's seat, where the envoy stands) · `passage_at_the_border` (a landing or a circle arrival, `settlement (B)`) · `armed_incursion` (a landing, `settlement (B)`) · `rescue_attempt` (the VENUE town where the captive is held) · `tax_refusal` (the town, its court and its square — an interior card, anchored at its one actor's own town) | the town where the act stands — its walls, its gate, its court; the anchor (`targetSaveId`) is the docket's key and the WHERE is the place, two separate fields that often, but not always, name one town |

| `site` | THE SITES' five kinds `site_taken` · `site_cleared` · `site_reclaim` · `site_siege` · `site_sale` (the site also rides the card's `subject` dial, never an actor ref), with a body on either side · CH's kinds and `person_at_the_gate` at a site · `bodies_meet` · `sponsorship_offered` · `passage_at_the_border` at THE SITES' `harbour_works`, once that record lands · `rescue_attempt` (with ES-2b, a body's captive at its den; a `route_node` hold is never a candidate); a party's reach of a site is a PT deed door, not a hearing | a world site, when the record lands |
