**THE ONE STRETCH KEY** (by delegation, vetoable; PR and PS state it, MN, CH and ST cite it): a ROAD is unordered, `road{a, b}`, and its pair is the digest's LANDED pair form — `spatialDigest.js`'s module-local `pairKey`, which joins the two ids in codepoint order with a bar, `a\|b` — so the stretch key is `stretch:<a\|b>`. A power's HALF of a road is the part on that power's own land, cut at the digest gate between the two territories (`spatialDigest.js`: the gate between two neighbours, "the cheapest crossing"); `gateKey(prev, B) = road{prev, B}` — the gate is that road's cut point, and B's half is the one a unit entering B's land walks; a named traveller on hop `hopIndex` at `progress01` stands on the origin's half while `progress01` is below the gate's fraction along the hop (`cut01`, DRAFT), then on the destination's. `cut01` is 0.5 on every road until the digest carries a gate fraction (its crossings hold `{ a, b, cost, cellA, cellB }` and no fraction — the §3.1 map INBOX (l)). A card's `road (a, b)` names the WHOLE road unless the kind names a half (a menace holds the whole road, MN §7; a patrol's turn-back is the riders' half, CH §18); cells and a world point are optional placement data, never asserted (H5's rule). `gateKey` is a parameter name only, and a homonym of a function-local in `display/defenseDisplay.js` (a readiness gate's key, never exported). `hashInt` → the landed `hash01` (`region/contestMath.js`) or `fnv1a32`; the sea-hop test is `roads/seaRoads.js :: classifyLegModes`; every volume that minted `a␟b` or `hashInt` aligns in its own text. THE DIAL EXCEPTION, by name: a road written into a VERB DIAL — MN's `RAISE_MENACE` `where` dial and ST's `PLACE_SITE` road value — is `a␟b`, the realm manifest's landed composite-target separator (`events/realmManifest.js :: COURSE_TARGET_SEP = '␟'`, split at the first separator into `targetId` and `courseKey`), read ONCE at the verb and split into the record's `where.ids: [a, b]` (MN §2's `RAISE_MENACE` row; ST §6.2) — NEVER a stored key, a pair key or a stretch key; every STORED pair is the digest's `a\|b` and every stretch key `stretch:<a\|b>`. This supersedes the ODQ §934.93 closing addition (b) ("`anchor␟a\|b`; no exception is minted") for these two dials; MN cites this paragraph.

**THE TRAIL AND THE JUNCTION ARE NO WHERE KINDS.** A hidden way runs beside the road whose junction is that stretch (BD §5.2), so a card on a trail names `road (a, b)` and the trail on its face; `trail:<a\|b>` is the hidden way's KEY beside `stretch:<a\|b>` (§5), never a WHERE value. A junction is a NAMED PLACE ON A ROAD — where a way meets a stretch (the map contract: "Where a path meets a road is a junction … Junctions have names"; "junctions are NAMED by the map and are the hearing card's WHERE (`junction:<id>`; ENC-HL-13)") — so a card at a junction names `road (a, b)` as its WHERE KIND and `junction:<id>` as its KEY, beside `stretch:<a\|b>` and `trail:<a\|b>`, with the junction's name on its face; never a sixth WHERE kind, and `junction:<id>` never a WHERE value (the closed list's walker would refuse it). Each junction lies on ONE stretch (BD §5.2: a corridor beside a road meets its own stretch, a bypass meets the stretches at its two ends), and the map is ASKED to confirm that a junction is a point on one primary hop, so its `a\|b` is the card's road (the §3.1 map INBOX (n)); THE PASSAGE's hidden-way catch keys `junction:<id>` under `road (a, b)`. `ENGAGEMENT_WHERE_KINDS` stays `settlement · road · country · site · border_segment`.

**The rulings on the registry** (by delegation, vetoable): (1) the registry and the PENDING LEDGER are built by ONE spine wave, **SP-9 THE HEARING WEEK** (the ledger shape, the HEARING SEAL kind (keyed by engagement id; engine · tilt · verdict), J-D7's adjudication screen as the pending panel, the held docket read-first at the next advance, the premium gate, the flag `hearingWeekEnabled`, the per-kind `registerEngagementKind` with a closure pin that grows only by registration), dark; (2) the first rows LIT are the THREE kinds already alive: `pact_acceptance` (the envoy's hearing included — `envoy_hearing` is merged into it), `siege` (seal 6 generalised), `armies_meet` (the live encounter); every other row lands with the wave that builds its kind, as an obligation of that wave's charter (§8). SP-9's charter line in `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_SP.md` ("The four live kinds register first: `pact_acceptance`, `envoy_hearing`, `siege`, `armies_meet`") and GR's registration bracket (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_GR.md`) owe the same correction by an insertion-only bracket; `DESIGN_FP_SPINE.md` carries no `envoy_hearing`; (3) once lit, a kind outside the registry cannot resolve — the resolver's split (docket-then-resolve) is enforced by a walker over every REGISTERED resolver, seeded from §3.4's census of live acts so it is green on day one; (4) SP-9 is THE HEAD OF THE CHAIN ORDER (§6): every later felt chain is felt through its hearings; (5) the pulseKernel stays frozen — the split lives in each resolver's own module, the ledger write in `spatialLedgers`, no pause cursor at all: the tick completes and the next advance resolves the docket first; J-D7's adjudication screen is the panel; the majors pause stays the DM's option (directive 7).

**ONE COLUMN ON EVERY REGISTERED CARD, `participation`:** every card a registered kind mints carries ONE `participation` slot — the paragons present at the card's place that week and what each did with its aim, stated as a deed in the world's words (CH §18's example: "Captain Vey is in Kell and has not taken up arms."), filled by THE CHAMPIONS' stage from `paragonsAt` and the paragon's `aim` for the week; never a forecast; it changes NO verdict, default or tilt; empty, the card shows no line; the scrutiny layer (on the card), never pushed. The column is the registry's: SP-9's card shape carries it and every owning volume's row inherits it without a cell of its own (the two newest kinds' cards included); `participation` is a prose word in 20 master `src` files with 0 quoted literals (`cure/D-CH-p5-probe/census-p5.out`) — a card field, registered in §5.

---

## §4 THE CLOCK — one law, one measured defect class, one walker, one owner row

**The law** is requirement 16 (§1, OD-FC-1 for the owner's word on the product rule). Its evidence, from the family reads (§7 of each family's file in the corpus read's kit): drama lives at the seasonal or yearly clock in every family — SP-C's posture (156 weeks), credibility's 52-week half-life, a spy's two-year expected life at `EXPOSE_BASE` 0.01, INT-5 and WC-15 at the epoch, R6's release standing on the occupation ladder, every war-close fold — while a DM advances weeks between sessions. The fast-layer sign each mechanism must declare is named per family in each volume's wave charters (the "FIRST-VISIBLE: …" line).

**The measured defect class** (findings WY §7, CONFIRMED by read):

| Class | What | Count | Cure |
|---|---|---|---|
| A — the 12-tick year | constants that assume a 12-tick year: `STARVE_FULL_TICKS = 12` ("a season"), `CREDIT_TERM: 12` ("a season or two"), ten unlabeled 12s | 12 | the two comment-mismatch values are same-seed shifts: OD-FC-15. The ten unlabeled 12s are measured one by one by the walker's first run; each is a cure row or an exemption with a reason |
| B — shadow clocks | local re-declarations of the interval grid (`WEEKS_PER_YEAR`, `SEASON_WEEKS = 13`, `tick / 52`, the kernel's inline table, two same-named `INTERVAL_WEEKS` shadows) | ~20 modules | each becomes an `INTERVAL_WEEKS` read where the file is not frozen; the frozen kernel's inline table is an exemption WITH the frozen-file reason |
| C — authored durations as bare literals | 13/26/52 hand-keyed in ~45 FP and world kernels (right values, underived) | ~60 constants | the walker ACCEPTS them when the file imports `INTERVAL_WEEKS` and the literal is a named constant whose comment names the season/half-year/year; it REDS a bare literal in an expression |

**The walker** (ruled by delegation, vetoable): requirement 9 already promised "a time-base walker"; building a promised instrument is REPAIR, not new capability. SP-7 (now freed of the mode table, §3.1.11) is re-chartered as `tests/lint/timeBase.walker.test.js`: a shrink-only exemption list seeded from the Class A/B/C census above, a RED on any new bare 12/13/26/52 in `src/domain` outside the list, zero behaviour. No constant moves in the walker's commit. It rides the next spine unit. The mode-speed table stays WY-1's.

**What a DM advance IS** (for the latency law): the `RealmForecast` advance units are week / month / season / year; the engine composes a year as 52 `one_week` ticks (EP findings §2.4, `advanceMultiTick`). First-visible latency is therefore counted in TICKS the DM can choose to see, and "a one-month advance" is four ticks.

**The planting line** (ruled by delegation under requirement 16, a rule rather than a question): a mechanism whose verdict arrives a war or a generation later (comradeship, veterans, diaspora bonds, introduction attempts, release standing, INT-5, WF-7's temple standing, the EP year anchor) declares as its FAST-LAYER SIGN a PLANTING LINE at the moment of cause — a dossier or Herald sentence that names the cause in the record's voice ("the Emberford company served beside Thornhold's through the winter siege") and nothing about the future. Under the Evidence Law it is complete (who, what, where) and not a hint; the slow verdict, when it comes, names the planting as its cause (requirement 20). The planting line is the FIRST-VISIBLE entry those waves carry in their charters.

---

## §5 THE VOCABULARY REGISTER — one word, one meaning per surface (requirement 21d; rulings by delegation; LANDED tokens never rename; UNLANDED tokens rename now unless three or more volumes key on the name, in which case the name stands with the register's note; PROSE disambiguates always)

**How to read this register.** One word carries one meaning on each surface (a code token, a persisted value, a DM sentence, a scrutiny page, the map). Each row names the WORD, the MEANINGS FOUND on the surfaces where it occurs, and the RULING. FREE = no literal on master `81f1cd963` in `src/` and `tests/` (a count is given where there are hits); LANDED = reused in its landed meaning only; HOMONYM = another meaning on another surface, registered and compound-scoped. Each program volume's own token register is of record for its census and its rows (PL §2 · MN §3.4 · RN §2.4 · CH §2.3 · PT §2.4 · PS §15 · ST §2.3 and §2.5 · PR §15.2 · BD §2.6 · OP §16 · MG §16 · WS §14 and §2.5 · FS §16 · PA §21); this register closes the list across volumes, names the cross-surface homonyms, and lists every WITHDRAWN name so that no text mints it again. Identifiers, flags and code values are machinery: none appears in a DM sentence, and a scrutiny word is shown in its prose form. The register runs in five parts: THE CORPUS WORDS; THE PROGRAM VOLUMES' TOKENS, one block per volume; THE MAP REGISTER; THE SHARED KEYS, LADDERS AND STORES; and the REINFORCE binding.

**The censuses of record.** Behind every FREE, LANDED and HOMONYM status stand the owning volume's census (named in its register) and the fold's own censuses at master `81f1cd963`: `cure/L-FOLD.md` §2 (the first spot census) · `cure/D-FOLD-r1-probe/tokens-r1.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-r2-probe/tokens-r2.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-r2b-probe/tokens-r2b-seat2.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-r3-probe/tokens-r3.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-r3-probe/receipts-r3.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-r4-probe/census-r4.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-r4-probe/census-r4b.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-r5-probe/census-r5.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-r5-probe/receipts-r5b.out` · `cure/L-FOLD2-probe/census-p4.out` · `assessments/P5b-probe/tokens-A.out` · `cure/D-FOLD-p5-probe/census-p5.out` · `cure/L-FOLD3-probe/census-p7.out` (cited below as `census-p7.out`, with its companions `census-p7b.out` and `census-p7c.out`). The volumes' censuses this register cites by name: `cure/L-*-probe/` (each lane's, `cure/L-<V>-probe/`) · `cure/D-PL-r1-probe/tokens.out` · `cure/D-PL-MN-RN-p5-probe/tokens-pl.out` · `cure/D-PL-r4.md` · `cure/D-MN-r1.md` §1(n) · `cure/D-RN-r5.md` §7 · `cure/D-CH-r1-probe/` · `cure/D-CH-r2-probe/tokens.out` · `cure/D-CH-r2b-probe/resume/census-r2b-resume.out` · `cure/D-CH-r5-probe/census-r5.out` · `cure/D-CH-p5-probe/census-p5.out` · `cure/D-CH-PD-r1-probe/census.out.tsv` · `cure/L-CH-PD-probe/census-pd.out.tsv` · `cure/D-ST-r3.md` (m2, R2) · `cure/D-ST-r4.md` · `cure/D-PS-r4.md` §5 · `cure/D-PR-r3-probe/tokens-r3.out` · `cure/D-PR-r4.md` · `cure/D-BD-p5-probe/census-p5.out` (with `census-p5b.out`) · `cure/D-OP-r1-probe/tokens-r1.out` · `cure/D-OP-r2-probe/tokens-r2.out` · `cure/L-OP-probe/tokens-2.out` · `cure/D-MG-r2-probe/census-r2.out` · `cure/D-MG-r3-probe/census-r3.out` · `cure/D-WS-r2.md` (N9) · `cure/D-WS-r3-probe/tokens-r3.out` · `cure/D-FOUR-r4-probe/tokens-ws.out` · `cure/D-FS-r1-probe/token-census-r1.txt` · `cure/D-FS-r2-probe/token-census-r2.txt`.

| `fealty_*` (PL; ST and BD request rows) | kinds `fealty_question` · `fealty_break`; the casus pair `fealty_broken` · `fealty_restored`; the decider `fealtyQuestion`; the record `relationshipState.fealtyRecord` (one row: `fealtyRow`) with rows `protection_kept · protection_failed · relief_sent · relief_kept · fealty_withheld · fealty_defied · service_answered · service_refused`, the SUCCESSION family's `fealty_sworn` (the baseline) and `fealty_renewed`, and the requested rows; who writes each row; `reliefAnswerOf` | FREE (0 src); the casus keeps `fealty_broken`, the hearing is `fealty_break`. THE FEALTY RECORD'S FAMILIES (PL §8, of record; OD-PL-52): OBLIGATION (`relief_sent · relief_kept · protection_kept · protection_failed · service_answered · service_refused`, and a refused arbitration's `fealty_defied`) under `polityObligationsEnabled`; SUCCESSION (`fealty_sworn · fealty_renewed · fealty_withheld`) under `politySuccessionEnabled`; BREAK (PL7's `fealty_defied`; a `fealty_break` verdict's own row) under `polityBreakEnabled`; REQUESTED (through `requestFealtyRow`) under the requester's flag ∧ `polityEnabled`. No flag writes the record whole, and each family's dark-after-lit is its own (PL §10). "`FEALTY_ROW_FAMILIES`" names PL §8's table only; PL mints no such identifier. TWO FACTS on every protection occasion (OD-PL-53): the ANSWER (`relief_sent · relief_kept`) carries the patch and moves trust, and it is MINTED at the week's resolution from the line as it stands, never at first sight (PL §4.3, OD-PL-54 · OD-PL-55); the OUTCOME (`protection_kept · protection_failed`) moves the hold's `P` and patches nothing — for an incursion, `protection_kept` when the member suffered no incursion harm and `protection_failed` when it did, whatever the answer was; the receipt says which ("sent, but too few"); grip moves by resentment, never by trust (PL's section WHAT MOVES THE OATH). `reliefAnswerOf` is PL's one pure, draw-free relief decider (PL §4.3), shared with MN-4 and called by THE PASSAGE's incursion leaf at FIRST SIGHT, its answer the incursion card's default during the pause. THE DEN'S OCCASION (ST §6.6, OD-ST-46): `den_unanswered` closes ONCE under `den_unanswered:<siteId>:<tick>` — `protection_kept` when the den is CLEARED within its one-season clock, by the liege's answer or by anyone, AND the site then stands unheld or held by a holder the member does not hold hostile (a hostile force that clears a den and keeps it leaves a den, so the occasion runs on); `protection_failed` when the clock EXPIRES with the den still holding, whether the liege came and was repelled or never came (the receipt says which: "Kell's lord came, but the robbers held the keep"); NO ROW when the den ends or leaves within the clock by its own course (no protection was tested); it never fires for a `keptSecret` den; both closings are REQUESTED rows under `siteFatesEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled`, never `polityObligationsEnabled`, so the two outcomes are symmetric under every legal flag set. THE PEACE (BD §10.2; PL §4.8, OD-PL-57): `peace_unkept` — a member's land exporting a body's trouble, the SAME word and meaning as BD's incident `peace_unkept` (the neighbour's resentment patch, a `bodyKind` field), an aligned pair of records of one fact family (the pair's incident · the fealty row) — is a REQUESTED row (PL §4.8 (1); PL §8 lists "THE BODIES' `peace_unkept` and harbouring (`body:`)"): BD requests it through `requestFealtyRow` (episode `body:`), and it is written under BD's `bodySpillEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled` (its liege face also needs `polityObligationsEnabled` by BD's own gate), with NO patch and never read by `P`; it is an obligation by MEANING only, the member's default, never the liege's, beside THE SITES' two den closings and THE BODIES' harbouring row. THE BANNER (§3.3.15): OBLIGATION's protection rows record whether the kingdom's net RODE for a levied member (riders placed = `protection_kept`); a lender's yearly credit is ONE `service_answered` row on PL's own episode `riders_lent:<rootId>:<yearStartTick>` (the lend episode `riders_lent:`; no registered prefix: PL mints it), and `riders_lent` is also PL's §15.9 incident word for the peacetime lend's wear; a sweep ordered unasked is precedence friction, a resentment patch. **THE MAP REGISTER** (the map module's "One word, one meaning" rule, in the owner's attachment `HOST-REQUIREMENTS-2026-10-18.md`: the map's meaning beside the FP corpus's, word by word; two surfaces, one meaning on each; prose qualifies)

| Word | Meanings found | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| `realm` | MAP: a polity drawn as a SHAPE ("realms as shapes with status, allegiance"; "the DM's realm names (christenings)"; "a third realm in a cell is 'outranked'") · FP: the campaign WORLD (RN's `FAME_SCOPES` `realm`, the rumor road's `RumorContent.scope`; MN's "realm scope" news) | the map's realm shape IS PL's polity drawn (H13's `christened` = PL's `polityNames`); FP never writes "realm" for a polity (the `polity` row); an FP card says "the crown of X" or "X's sworn towns", the map's legend "realm" |
| `sway` | MAP: its own owner-ruled derivation on the "allegiance" lens, never host truth · FP: once PL's criminal-polity prose noun | the word is the MAP's; PL's criminal noun is "X's ring" (PL §2); FP never writes "sway" |
| `marker` · `dated fact` | MAP: its plague approach ring ("the weeks to onset"), its hop links and its columns "toward their arrival", captioned with weeks (its M23) · FP: WY §3b THE AMBIGUITY LAW ("A marker is a presence, never an intention") | the law's object is the MOVER MARKER: an army, a column, a caravan or a named traveller is a presence on every surface, the map module's included — no arrow, destination line, path highlight, heading or ETA; a DATED FACT the host computed (an arrival or onset week, a hop's weeks, a column's dispatch and arrival weeks) is a RECEIPT and may carry its week on any surface, captioned as the host's dated fact ("arrived week n"), never as a destination the mover intends; a likely road laid under a column is DM-only and labelled; WY's bracket lands at WY's next unit (§3.1.38) |
| `allegiance` | MAP: H19's lens that draws the sway · FP: PL's `allegiance_choice` (a member's answer after a polity claim, `crown · claimant · aside`) | two surfaces; the map's lens never names a PL hearing; the PL card says the member "takes a side" |
| `claimant` | HOST: the heir word (`heir.js`, landed) · MAP: refused ("outranked", never "claimant") · PL: a docket VERDICT word (`allegiance_choice`'s `claimant`; `claim_prevails`) | the host's heir sense stands; PL's is a docket verdict naming the side that pressed a polity claim (the same family sense, one who claims a seat), never drawn on the map |
| `gate` | MAP: the digest gate, "never written bare" · FP: the town's gate (CH's `person_at_the_gate`, PT's `party_gate`, `gateDefault`), a stressor gate (`STRESSOR_SPAWN_GATES`), the owner's gate list (push · merge · deploy · migration · dispatch) | never bare on a new surface: "the town's gate", "a stressor gate", "the digest gate", "the gate list"; kind and leaf names stand |
| `lens` | MAP: a map layer (territory, allegiance, plague …) · FP: the §934.87 borders lens (a map layer, the map's sense) and the causal lens (the `partyView` row); RN's two READINGS of a deed are never lenses (its code token `lensOne` keeps its name) | on every DM surface a lens is a map layer; RN's readings are "readings" in prose, never "lenses"; the cause walk is "the Why panel" |
| `hold` | MAP: a march's "hold band" · FP: PL's `polityHold` (the crown's hold), MN's hold of a stressor or of might (`menaceHolds`, `mightHeld`), the strategy move `hold`, `HABIT_HOLD_BANDS`, the held docket, MG's `hold` verb (PS's march hold) | compound identifiers only; prose qualifies ("the crown's hold", "the march's hold band"); PT mints no `hold` |
| `warden` | MAP and H13: "wardens' terms" (route wardenship) · FP: EC-6's `route_wardenship` stance term (OD-FC-2) · CH: a DISPLAY noun for a `wildland` paragon (CH16) · BD: the holder of a town's PUBLIC-SECURITY office (the `law_enforcement` senior seat, or the sword faction's leader seat) when that holder is a `wildland` paragon (CH's `isWardenParagon`) | ONE ROW, NO PICK (by delegation, vetoable): `route_wardenship` is the term, and no code token is the bare word — the rule reads the paragon's `domain` and the OFFICE, never the word (master's `roles/roleCatalog.js :: INSTITUTION_ROLES` has no warden office); CH's warden is a person's display noun and never names a term; prose always qualifies — "route wardenship" (the trade term), "the warden" only for the paragon who holds the town's watch, and "the watch" for the office itself |
| force band · strength order · `might` · `homeDefense` | MAP and H13: a seat's force band 0–4 and garrison presence (new host state) and its strength order "banded in fifths" · MN: a menace's `might` (`STRENGTH_BAND_KEYS` on the 0..100 latent scale) · WAR: `homeDefense` (`warCapacityReads.js`, the one defence read a siege verdict faces) · PL: a polity's believed strength share | FOUR QUANTITIES, never equated (§3.1.29); "force" is the map's word only; a menace siege's card names `homeDefense` as its read. A SEAT's force band and garrison presence (H13's `PolitySeat.military.forceBand` and `.garrisonPresent`) are a SLOT, whose likely owner is the war volume's capacity facets (the host document's J384 (4)), and they do NOT read THE SITES; THE SITES answer only H13's `PolitySite`, for HELD sites, with the site's READINESS word (`garrisonBandOf` → `SITE_READINESS`, §3.1.29), which is neither a force band nor a fifth strength quantity on the army's scale |
| `patrol` | MAP: its own picture of patrols, never drawn lowering danger or holding a quarantine line until H25 gives it a meaning · FP: THE PATROLS (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PR.md`, dark behind `patrolsEnabled` and `patrolEffectsEnabled`; its ledger `spatialLedgers.patrols`) | Patrol coverage's PRODUCER is PR (OD-FC-2, the WC INBOX (3) and the map INBOX (f) read so). ONE ENTRY: `patrolCounterTerm` (the deviation of a town's watch from its tier's reference watch) enters THE SITES' one post-blend slot after `counterforceAssessment` (through ST's leaf `siteTermAt`; sites and patrols sum under ST's one clamp), and a raiding menace's wear takes it ONLY THROUGH THE STAMP (`stressors.js :: ageRoamingStressors` stamps "`counterforce: assessment`", the score MN §5.5's wear read takes — MN's slot (2) "FILLED THROUGH THE STAMP, never added here"); PR adds no row to `menaceCounterTerms`, so the riders are never counted twice — "two consumers, one value" in exactly this sense; patrols also SUBTRACT on the one presence term of the danger ledger, enforce the border (THE PASSAGE) and lower escort risk (WY-6) — one truth each. GOODS are caught at the route's WORST GATE (`smuggle.js :: smuggleSuccessChance`, one optional watch input, the gate town's OWN riders — J385), never at the roads gauntlet, which rolls hazards for missions only and takes no line (`roadsKernel.js` frozen; the slow half alone for missions now, the fast half a C7 slot). Under the owner's standing words ("no just build it all shipped lit" 09-23; "focus primarily on the ui and the prose first and then everything else after" 10-02; FP-40 CHAIN-FIRST), nothing below retires a wave. Every wave not in a felt chain keeps its charter and gains a SLOT (the never-deferred law): its chain's name. | C8 · THE AGES (SP-8 / J-D12, OD-FC-17) | THE AGES ARE THE MACRO CHURN, MADE LEGIBLE OVER LONG SPANS (OD-FC-73): kingdoms are made and unmade; settlements are raised, promoted, demoted, destroyed and re-founded; the map's shape moves with its resources; the technology stands still — MEDIEVAL FOR MILLENNIA, no technological eras: SP-F's era marking names spans, never a technology (OD-FC-70); heirs and reigns are not the ages' substance — NO ENGINE MORTALITY CLOCK for named persons, so the age receipt's thinness on heirs, long reigns and turnover is BY DESIGN and is said plainly in the receipt (lifespan is the DM's through species and character; death stays the DM's verdict; OD-FC-69) — towns and crowns are. The age layer and the ERA-PRESET-ELIGIBLE marking carry it. THE PIECES THAT EXIST, cited and never duplicated: the urban band's tier moving with prosperity (the owner's 09-30 word; promotion and demotion landed); POP's founding and death of settlements, the remnant, the site that outlives its town (ST14); PL's kingdom formation, precedence, feud · break · polity war and the capital as apex; EC's resource base; roads rising and decaying (ENC-HP-13; WY); plague waves (PG) and war exhaustion (WC). The read's felt-value judgments stay in its files as the experience reads' hypotheses.

**THE ORDER NOTE** (§7 OD-FC-56; by delegation, vetoable — a re-sequencing of a program the owner sequenced under FP-40, so VISIBLE in ODQ §934.93). It is THE DEFAULT A READ MAY OVERTURN: OD-FC-19 stands, and each chain is CHOSEN at the previous chain's experience read, never written ahead; C0.5's re-sequencing is the owner's to veto (OD-FC-54). THE INSTRUMENTS FIRST (the paragraph below — before C0, and before the release if the owner so decides) → C0 THE HEARING WEEK → C0.5 THE CAUSE WALK → C1 THE STATECRAFT BAND → C15's FORT-FIRST CHAIN (ST-0 → ST-1, keeping its place on the owner's 01:00 word) → the shared head unit of C11 and C13 (RN-0 + PT-0) → RN-1 → RN-2 → RN-3 → PT-1 → PT-2 → C12 THE CHAMPIONS → INT's TWO CHEAP HOPS, brought forward (C4's INT-3a and INT-7; the panel names no exact place, and they are placed after C12 — vetoable) → GENERALS UNIT 0 (the cure of the march), before ANY wilds chain → C9 THE CROWN and C10 THE MENACE, each keeping its first felt chain → C14 THE PASSAGE and C16 THE PATROLS only behind the map's M-W2 AND the war layer's lighting (they do nothing in the default preset), C16 adjacent after C15 → C17 THE BODIES after C15 and C16 → the rest (C2..C8). C22 THE REALM'S PAST (§7 OD-FC-131; PA §17, OD-PA-20) is ELIGIBLE after THE INSTRUMENTS BEFORE C0 — it needs their determinism check and their per-tick budget, and THE BIRTH DOOR for PA-2 on a plain birth — and it needs nothing from C0, holding no hearing; it is CHOSEN at a read under FP-40 like every chain, and the recommendation of record is the read after C0, because it lights the first hour of every born world; it is itself a world-birth change, ordered among the release's world-birth changes after the instruments, and its slots ride their owners' chains (PL's at C9, POP's at C3, RN's at C11).

WHY THIS ORDER. C11 precedes C12 and C13 because both read it; C13 and C12 come before the polity's deeper waves because the fast actors are the couplings that bring the slow systems to the table (OD-FC-55). C11 and C13 share one head unit (RN-0 + PT-0), and RN-2 joins C11's first felt chain before C13's PT-1 and PT-2; PT-1 and PT-2 follow RN-3, not RN-2 alone: PT REGISTERS its inherent-value rows at RN-3's unit (RN §6.1's registration door), and without RN-3 no reading of a tale exists for PT-1 to show or PT-2 to answer, so the first felt chain is (RN-0 + PT-0) → RN-1 → RN-2 → RN-3 → PT-1 → PT-2 (RN-3 carries no lighting gate); RN-4's place within C11 is unchanged. THE PASSAGE, THE SITES and THE PATROLS share the border gate and the fort, so C16 stays adjacent after C15; C15's fort-first chain keeps its early place, while C14 and C16 wait behind the map's M-W2 and the war layer's lighting because they do nothing in the default preset.

**THE PROGRAM'S LATER CHAINS** (§7 OD-FC-123; by delegation, vetoable), each THE DEFAULT A READ MAY OVERTURN (each chain CHOSEN at the previous chain's experience read): CH-11 THE PATRON DEITY rides C12 (after CH-1 · CH-5 and RN-3's two exports); C18 THE OPPORTUNITY follows C0 and C11's RN-1 with RN's rows first, its rescue wave after ES-2b's custody wave and its foreign-guest arms after WR-7d's persistence; C19 THE MOVEMENT GRAMMAR follows C14's PS-0 and its lit march row (so it waits, with C14, behind the map's M-W2 and the war layer's lighting) and GENERALS UNIT 0; C20 THE WAY OF WAR's leaning waves follow `WC-car-0`, C4's first wave and C19's first wave, inside the war chain, while its WS-0/WS-1 may run once the war layer's chain is lit; C21 THE FISCAL SEAT follows C3, INT-2 and W-COIN-2's chip — the panel's "build the instruments and the interior before a tenth wilds volume" (OD-FS-21) is answered by C21's prerequisites, never by a jump: FS-1 cannot precede C3 and INT-2. THE INSTRUMENTS BEFORE C0 (below) stand unchanged by them; the program's own instruments are car-0s inside their chains (WS-0's `wayOfWarCensus.mjs`, OP-8, FS-0, MG-0), none a chain of its own.

**THE SHARED PRESENCE COMPOSER** (`spatial/presenceComposer.js :: presenceTerms`, §3.1.30) is MN's OWNED unit (§3.3.16 (1)): it lands immediately before the first wave in this order that registers a feeder — on this default, before ST-1 — never "first to land". The waves that need it are PS-3 · ST-1 · PR-1 · MN-1 (PS §10, ST §9, OD-PR-43, MN §3.3), and THE BODIES' BD-2 registers the bodies' row as one more feeder on the composer's closed list (BD §8.2), never a second composition. A read that re-orders the chains moves the composer's place with them, never the composition, which stays MN §3.3's one sentence.

**THE INSTRUMENTS BEFORE C0** (§7 OD-FC-78; by delegation, vetoable) — the first build's PREREQUISITES. They are CODE, recorded here as the order's head: (1) THE BIRTH DOOR at its re-confirmed size (+30 keys; OD-FC-62, by delegation on the owner's "i leave to your recommendation", re-confirmed at its unit); (2) OD-FC-32's PERSISTED UNDO; (3) an AGGREGATE PER-TICK COST BUDGET across all lit stages at continent settlement counts; (4) a PUSH-BUDGET HARNESS across ALL lit chains together; (5) the whole-world soak (`scripts/audit/whole-world-soak.mjs`) EXTENDED FROM 30 TO 100 YEARS, answering NINE QUESTIONS per run and per period — did anything meaningful change (the world-state delta); what (the event and provenance record); why (the cause chain); how far it propagated (hop count); its downstream effects (dependency activation); instability (the runaway metric, the ceiling); stasis (the dynamism metric, the floor — consequential chains, OD-FC-77); determinism (same-seed re-runs byte-identical); player relevance (the docket and stakes count per week); (6) the DEED-RECORDING SURFACE's CONTRACT, specified in RN-0/PT-0's packet. The recommendation, by delegation, is to build them BEFORE THE RELEASE, because they measure the landed world too; that timing is THE OWNER'S GATE (the dispatch gate is the release, pushed and deployed). Nothing dispatches by this paragraph.

**THE MAP PREREQUISITES** — the world canon is the concrete carrier of every "physical world is a seam" arm, so the map module's milestones are prerequisites of the chains: for each chain, the host-change rows (H-rows: the map document's only host-change list) that are THE SAME UNIT as an FP wave (one unit, one writer — never two lanes building one field), and the map milestone a map-facing wave waits on (M-W1 the world · M-W2 routes, polity, siting stage 2 · M-W3 labels, exports, the adapter · M-W5 the lenses; read from the map document's build-order table, PLAUSIBLE where a milestone is inferred from a lens ask's place in J384 (8)). THE FLAG-SERIALIZATION NOTE: the host lights its flags in dependency order as separate lighting units, and flag-minting commits serialize across the host's volumes (the map document's "lighting-order trap"), so an H-row that mints a flag is never built in the same worktree at the same time as an FP wave minting another; each pair below lands in one lane or in sequence. THE BODIES' row rests on the map's reply on the hidden ways (the kit's `refs/MAP-REPLY-HIDDEN-WAYS-2026-10-18.md`). Every H-row of the map document, H1–H30 — and so every row below — is ACCEPTED WORK by the owner's word (2026-10-18 05:57 EDT, verbatim: "6. i told the mapmaker i do as it suggested."), and H31–H45, the rows the map program's experience pass added (its J397 host prompts of 2026-10-24), by the owner's word of 2026-10-24, verbatim: "accept everything" (OD-FC-135: on H1–H30's terms, accepted and NOT scheduled, each built inside the FP unit that owns its field; H35–H44's text asked of the map, §3.1.38), and the milestones M-W1 · M-W2 · M-W3 · M-W5 are PREREQUISITES of the map-facing waves of C7 · C9 · C10 · C12 · C13 · C14 · C15 · C16 · C17 (one unit, one writer per shared H-row); OD-FC-66 records what the word does NOT settle (J385's bounded arm — one confirmation asked).

## §7 THE OWNER ROWS — RULED BY DELEGATION (the owner's word of 2026-10-16, after reading the first reads and the fold's shape: "i want you to rule on the decisions. i give my permission now and for whatever is emergent")

**How to read this section.** Every row below was compiled as the owner's to decide. On the word above, each row's ruling column IS THE RULING, taken under delegation and VETOABLE by one owner clause — the same standing the ODQ gives every delegated ruling. The delegation reaches "whatever is emergent": every later row is ruled the same way and recorded in the ODQ — OD-FC-47 onward and the amendments of OD-FC-19, OD-FC-45 and OD-FC-46 under the owner's word of 2026-10-17, verbatim: "I defer all judgement and decisions to you and give you permissions needed now and those that will be emergent in the future"; the rows of 2026-10-20 under the owner's words "I give all permissions now and in the future" and "i defer everything to your judgemetn". A row marked THE OWNER'S — an OWNER ROW, an owner lighting row, or a ⭐ shape declared for the owner's confirmation at its unit — is NOT ruled by delegation: it is VISIBLE in the ODQ and re-confirmed in chat at its unit. Two things no word moves: nothing here DISPATCHES a build (the owner's release gate — UI + prose first, the EC gate "pushed and deployed" — binds), and the gate list (push · merge · deploy · migration · dispatch), with every golden re-record, still happens at its own unit with its own re-confirmation in chat. A ruling that moves a same-seed output (OD-FC-3, 5, 7, 12, 13, 15, 16, 18, 22, 26, 30, 33, 34, 39, 47, 48, 49, 57, the OWNER LIGHTING ROW OD-FC-61, and §3.2.3's reader) is therefore a SIGNED DESIGN whose shift is recorded when its lighting or cure unit lands, never before. Each row shows its final text only; the Ruled column carries its date and standing and, where a later ruling changed it, "(amended <date>)".
